# flare-v1-bugs

## Goal

Every bug the docs e2e app (`packages/core/tests/e2e/apps/docs`) found in `packages/core/src` is fixed with a failing test written first, and the docs app runs on every project with no console error or warning. Each phase below is one bug: its repro, what was expected, what happened, and the evidence. An engineer agent takes one phase at a time.

## Context

- The tester writes the app and the specs. Engineers change `packages/core/src` only. The app is not changed to hide a bug.
- Run the app in dev: `bun run --bun --cwd packages/core/tests/e2e/apps/docs dev`. Node 22.12 cannot load the raw-TS plugin, so dev needs `--bun`.
- Genuine Node runs need a Node with type stripping on PATH, for example `PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.21.0/bin:$PATH bunx playwright test …`. Under `bunx --bun`, the spawned `node` is Bun (B24).
- The Playwright specs are `packages/core/tests/e2e/docs-*.test.ts`. One project: `bun run test:e2e -- --project node-dev-chromium`.
- The contract is the exported source. A dx row that the source does not export is out of v1, not a bug.
- The rules are `blueprint/00-rules.md`: TDD, a colocated unit test, 100% per-file coverage, and file limits.

## Constraints

- A fix lands with its unit or integration test, seen failing first. The named docs spec case turns green.
- Do not edit the docs app to work around a bug. If the app is wrong, the tester fixes the app and moves the phase to Notes in the tester log.
- A phase is closed when its Verify leaf passes.

## Watch-outs

- Hydration bugs cascade: one key miss detaches every later sibling, so fix 3 and 2 before judging the rest of a page.
- Several symptoms only show under `bun`. Run the dev repro under `--bun`, and the prod repro on the `bun-prod` project.

## Steps

1 [x] B1 Bun: a middleware that re-wraps the response crashes the server
1.1 [x] Repro: a middleware `async (ctx) => { const r = await ctx.next(); return new Response(r.body, r); }` under Bun dev, then GET any page. The process exits: `TypeError: Cannot cancel a locked ReadableStream` at `packages/core/src/server/middleware/run.ts:70` (`discard`). Two nested re-wraps reproduce it on the first request.
1.2 [x] Expected: the re-wrap is the pattern the comment at `run.ts:68` endorses, and the response streams. Actual: under Bun, `new Response(res.body, res).body` is a different stream object, so `routed.body !== kept.body` and `discard` cancels the locked original. The rejection is unhandled and kills the process.
1.3 [x] Write a failing `server/middleware/run.test.ts` case for a re-wrapped body whose `body` identity differs from the router's (simulate Bun). Then fix `discard` so it never cancels a stream that was handed on, and never throws or rejects unhandled.
1.4 [x] Verify: the docs app's `x-docs-time` middleware written as a re-wrap serves `/` under `bun run --bun ... dev`, and `docs-server` `middleware next` is green on `bun-prod-*`.

2 [x] B2 `useNavigationProgress` breaks hydration of every later sibling
2.1 [x] Repro: in the docs root layout, `<ProgressBar />` (which calls `useNavigationProgress`) sits before `<Header />`. Load `/` in dev and look at the console.
2.2 [x] Expected: no hydration warning. Actual: `Hydration tag mismatch for key …020401: expected <div>`, then `Hydration key miss` for the header, `#app`, the page, and the footer, then `Hydration completed with 67 unclaimed server-rendered node(s)`. `packages/core/src/client/navigation/progress.ts:27` returns `{ pending: false, value: null }` on the server before `useNavigation()`, `createSignal`, and `createEffect`, so the client allocates owners that the server did not, and every later hydration id shifts.
2.3 [x] Write a failing hydration test (SSR the hook beside a sibling element, hydrate, and assert no key miss). Then make the server path create the same owners as the client, or none on either side.
2.4 [x] Verify: `docs-smoke` `every page hydrates` reports no `Hydration` warning on `node-dev-chromium`.

3 [x] B3 A layout's boundary renders are created on the client at hydrate
3.1 [x] Repro: the docs root layout declares `.errorRender(...)` and `.notFoundRender(...)`. Load any page in dev.
3.2 [x] Expected: a boundary render runs only when its failure happens. Actual: `Hydration key miss for "000000000000000010010": no server-rendered element carries this key (template: <main …><h1 id=root-error>`. Every node after it then detaches: the footer never hydrates (the isomorphic badge stays "server" and the boot log stays empty), and the changelog's `"stream"` defers never fill. Removing both boundary renders from the root makes all three work. The same key miss appears for a non-root layout: on every doc page, the docs layout's `notFoundRender` template (`<div id=docs-not-found>`) is created at hydrate, which detaches the article that follows. This was verified by deleting them and probing `/` and `/changelog`. The boundary code is `packages/core/src/shared/components/boundary.ts`. The root slot is under `<Errored>` (`renderSlot.slot › <SlotContext> › <Errored>` in the warning's owner path).
3.3 [x] Write a failing `shared/components/outlet.test.tsx` (or boundary) case: SSR plus hydrate a root with `errorRender` and no failure, and assert no key miss and no fallback template created. Then fix it so the fallback is created lazily.
3.4 [x] Verify: `docs-data` `defer two skeletons` is green on node-dev-chromium, and `docs-smoke` `every page hydrates` shows no Hydration warning on any page; its remaining failures are B4, B5, and B7. Originally: `docs-smoke` `every page hydrates` and `docs-data` `defer two skeletons` are green with the root boundaries in place.
3.5 [x] Cause found: B2's cascade, not a boundary bug. The boundary renders were never eager. During hydrate, the key shift that starts at `<ProgressBar />` makes the root's subtree throw (`Hydration Mismatch. Unable to find DOM nodes for hydration key: …A1c10000k060` at depth 1, then a null `nextSibling` in `Controls`). The slot's `<Errored>` in `outlet.tsx` `guard` catches that throw and paints the root's `errorRender` on the client, so its template is the key miss. With `<ProgressBar />` removed from the docs root (a local experiment, reverted), `/`, `/docs/routing`, and `/changelog` hydrate with no Hydration warning, the footer reads "rendered on client", and the boot log fills. No `outlet.tsx` change: the B2 fix closes it, and `tests/integration/hydrate.test.tsx` `root boundaries idle` (chromium, firefox, webkit) is its regression case.

4 [x] B4 Data hooks warn `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` wherever they are called
4.1 [x] Repro: call `useParams({ from })`, `useSearch`, `useLoaderData`, `usePreloaderContext`, or `useMatch` in a component body (docs `Header` via `useLocaleParam`, `HooksPanel`, `Results`). Load the page in dev.
4.2 [x] Expected: hooks are called in a component body, so the call is silent. Actual: `[STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED] Reactive value "createRouterStore.snapshot" read directly in <Header> will not update`. `packages/core/src/shared/components/outlet-nodes.ts:119` (`matchOf`) calls `read()` once at the hook call, which reads `state()` untracked, and `live()` (`:126`) reads it again.
4.3 [x] Write a failing case that mounts a component calling each hook under Solid dev and asserts no `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`. Then check that the route is on screen without an untracked reactive read (for example `untrack`, or a check inside the proxy's first access).
4.4 [x] Verify: `docs-smoke` `every page hydrates` has no `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` warning.
4.5 [x] The five hooks are fixed: `matchOf`'s on-screen check and `live`'s first value read untracked (`tests/integration/hydrate.test.tsx` `data hooks read untracked`, chromium, firefox, webkit). On node-dev-chromium, `docs-smoke` `every page hydrates` now shows one STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED, on `/search?q=route` only, and it is the app's: the search page's render runs `createSignal(props.search.q)`, a one-time read of a reactive prop in the component body, so the draft would not follow a later `q`. The tester fixes the app (Constraints), then 4.4 can pass.

5 [x] B5 `.head({ meta: { charset } })` warns on the client
5.1 [x] Repro: the docs root `.head()` sets `meta.charset: "utf-8"`, a documented head field. Load any page in dev.
5.2 [x] Expected: no warning. Actual: `useHead: <meta> (charset) is shell-only and ignored on the client`, from the client head apply passing charset to `useHead`.
5.3 [x] Write a failing `client/head/apply.test.ts` case (charset in the merge, no `useHead` call for it on the client). Then emit charset in the server shell only.
5.4 [x] Verify: no `useHead` warning in `docs-smoke`.

6 [x] B6 The dev overlay opens for a deferred rejection that `<Errored>` caught
6.1 [x] Repro: `/changelog`'s `broken` defer rejects with `The stats service is down` under `<Errored>`. Load it in dev after B3 is fixed, or with the root boundaries removed.
6.2 [x] Expected (`dx/defer.md`, `dx/navigate.md`): a slot inside `<Errored>` does not send Vite's overlay payload. Actual: Vite's overlay opens with that error and its stack, while the `<Errored>` fallback also renders.
6.3 [x] Cause: the server projected every `e` frame through `framed` with the dev sink, so a deferred rejection in a data or fill answer sent the overlay payload, which the dev handler broadcast (B38, B42), whether or not an `<Errored>` on the client caught it. Seen: the overlay payload arrived over the websocket right after the document's `?_flare=fill`. Fix: `dataBody` marks a deferred value's `e`, and `framed` projects it with no send; it is still logged, and a loader `e` still sends. A deferred rejection no app boundary catches reaches the B77 walk and is reported from the client (B42). Failing test first: `answers.test.ts` `deferred no overlay`.
6.4 [x] Verify: `docs-data` `defer rejects`, `stream fill`, `stream`, and `stream abort` are green 3 of 3 on node-dev-chromium, with no `vite-error-overlay`.

7 [x] B7 `flare({ fonts: { dir } })` 404s every font in dev
7.1 [x] Repro: the docs config sets `fonts: { dir: "assets/fonts" }` and the root renders `<FontCSS font={inter} />`. Load `/` in dev.
7.2 [x] Expected: the font files the build emits are also served in dev, at the same registry URL. Actual: `Failed to load resource: 404` for `/fonts/inter/latin.woff2`, the preload, on every page. `packages/core/src/vite/plugin/fonts.ts:23` applies to the client build only (`generateBundle`). It has no dev middleware.
7.3 [x] Write a failing `vite/plugin/fonts.test.ts` case: a dev server with `fonts.dir` answers the registry URL with the woff2 bytes. Then add the dev serve. Fix: `fontsPlugin` gains `configureServer`, a middleware that answers a GET or HEAD for a registry file url from `dir` (`font/woff2`, `no-cache`), and passes anything else, a missing file included, to the app. The url check is emit.ts's `fontFile`, shared with the build. Failing tests first: `fonts.test.ts` `dev serve`, `dev fallthrough`.
7.4 [x] Verify: `docs-features` `fonts` and every `docs-smoke` page are green on node-dev-chromium with no 404. A full node-dev-chromium run went from 23 failures to 6: B21, B22, B23, B29, B39.4, and one `abort keeps latest` hydration timeout that passed 5 of 5 alone.

8 [x] B8 Dev HTML transform gets a partial document
8.1 [x] Repro: in dev, load `/api/server/create-server`, a shared `ssr` page under a segment with `cdn`, after one prior load.
8.2 [x] Expected: the dev handler buffers through `</head>` and transforms a well-formed head (`dx/plugin.md`). Actual: the terminal prints `Unable to parse HTML; parse5 error code eof-in-tag at …/apps/docs/api/server/create-server:1:9916`, pointing mid-attribute inside `<body>` (`…dark:hover:text-white opacity-`). The page still answers 200.
8.3 [x] Cause: `vite/dev/handler.ts` read until the text held `</head>`, then handed all of it to `transformIndexHtml`, including whatever body text came in the same chunk, so a chunk ending mid-tag after `</head>` reached parse5 as `eof-in-tag`. At the same boundary, the rest of the stream went out as raw bytes without flushing the decoder, so a multibyte character split there lost its first byte. Fix: the transform gets the document through `</head>` exactly, the rest of that text follows it, and the tail goes through the same decoder, flushed at the end. Failing tests first: `handler.test.ts` `head split`, `utf8 boundary`, and `streamed html` (which asserted the old input).
8.4 [x] Verify: the e2e boot keeps its runtime's output (`boot.test.ts` `logs captured`), and `docs-smoke` `dev log clean` loads every page of the route map on the dev projects with no `Unable to parse`, no uncaught error, and only the declared flare errors. The exact shape no longer fires at HEAD (a hit body is one string, a miss's first chunk the whole shell), so the guard was shown to bite: a transform fed a split tag fails it with `eof-in-tag`. All of docs-smoke, docs-cache, and the other node-dev specs pass but `format message` (B29) and `click through` (B39.4), which fail the same at HEAD.

9 [x] B9 Route builder types do not enforce method order
9.1 [x] Repro: `createRootLayout("_admin_").headers(...).security(...).head(...)` type-checks under the app tsconfig, then throws `order: head` from `packages/core/src/shared/route-builder/chain.ts:38` on the first request (a 500 on `/admin`).
9.2 [x] Expected (`dx/builder.md`): a method called after a later one is absent from the type. Actual: `head` stays on the type after `headers` and `security`. The same holds for `createPage("_admin_/admin").authenticate().preloader(...).authorize(...)`: it type-checks, then throws `order: authorize`. Because prerender imports every route module, that one wrong order fails the whole production build, even for a page that is never prerendered.
9.3 [x] Write a failing `tests/types/propagation.test.ts` case (`method order`: `head` after `headers` is `@ts-expect-error`). Then remove earlier methods from `RouteBuilder` as each step is called (`shared/route-builder/route-types.ts:158`).
9.4 [x] Verify: `bun run typecheck` passes with the new negative case. Fixed in 4457caf. Its verify waited on a green typecheck, which B25 and B37 give.

10 [x] B10 The sitemap drops every non-SSG page under an optional locale root
10.1 [x] Repro: the docs app's main root is `[[locale]]/_root_`. Build with `prerender.sitemap` and read `client/sitemap.xml`.
10.2 [x] Expected: a public page whose only param is the absent optional locale (`/search`, `/changelog`, `/playground`, `/examples`, `/[flare]`) is one entry, as a URL with no param segment is (`dx/seo.md`). Actual: 40 entries, all SSG docs and home pages plus `additionalEntries`. Every page that is not built at prerender is missing, so an i18n app's sitemap is SSG-only.
10.3 [x] Decided (B101): an absent optional param counts as resolved. Cause: prerender gave a route a `path` only when its pattern had no param segment, and the sitemap otherwise listed only prerendered paths, so every page under `[[locale]]/_root_` that is not prerendered was dropped. Fix: `PrerenderRoute.bare` is the URL with every optional segment absent, plus one per other configured tag when the locale param is among them (the tags come with the locale slot); the sitemap lists `path`, else rendered paths and `bare`, each once. A required param stays prerender-only. Failing tests first: `sitemap.test.ts` `optional absent`, `prerender.test.ts` `bare path`.
10.4 [x] Verify: `docs-features` `sitemap` lists `/search` and `/hr/search` and excludes `/admin`, and `robots` passes, on node-prod-chromium.

11 [x] B11 `public/` is copied into the server and worker outputs
11.1 [x] Repro: build the docs app and list the output.
11.2 [x] Expected: public files ship once, with the client. Actual: `server/` and `worker/` each also hold `favicon.ico`, `icon.svg`, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `icons/*`, `manifest.webmanifest`, and `fonts/mono.woff2`. The worker bundle uploads them as script assets for nothing.
11.3 [x] Cause: Vite copies `public/` into every environment's `outDir` unless `build.copyPublicDir` is false, and the `ssr` and `worker` builds left it on. Fix: both set `copyPublicDir: false`; the client build still copies it. Failing test first: `vite/plugin/environments.test.ts` `public once` (the three environments' config, and a real build with `public/x.txt` writes it under `client/` only).
11.4 [x] Verify: the docs app's prod output holds `favicon.ico`, `icon.svg`, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `manifest.webmanifest`, and `fonts/mono.woff2` under `client/` only, and each is served 200 on Node and on Workers prod; `docs-smoke`, `fonts`, and `head full` pass on node-prod-chromium and workers-prod-chromium (38 of 38).

12 [x] B12 A route `.security()` makes the CSP nonce differ from the document's, and the page never hydrates
12.1 [x] Repro: the docs `_admin_` root layout has `.security({ "X-Frame-Options": "DENY" })`. On a production build (`node-prod`), GET `/admin` as a guest (401), as `docs.session=bob.reader` (403), and as `docs.session=ada.admin` (200).
12.2 [x] Expected: one nonce per document (`dx/server.md` One nonce). Actual, every time: the header has `script-src 'self' 'nonce-741e…'` while the body's six inline tags carry `nonce="3347…"`. Chromium blocks every inline script and style (`Executing inline script violates … script-src`), and `html[data-flare-hydrated]` never appears. Main-root pages, which have no route `.security()`, match. `packages/core/src/server/security/apply.ts:20` calls `buildCsp` per apply, and `buildCsp` makes a fresh nonce. A route config re-applies after render with a new one.
12.3 [x] Write a failing `tests/integration/nonce.test.ts` case (`route security keeps nonce`): a root with `.security({ "X-Frame-Options": "DENY" })` has a CSP header nonce equal to every body nonce. Then reuse the request's nonce when applying the route config.
12.4 [x] Verify: `docs-server` `route security` and `unauthenticated` are green on `node-prod-chromium`, and `/admin` hydrates.

13 [x] B13 A root layout's `.security()` header override is not applied
13.1 [x] Repro: same as 12.1. The server sets `security: { "X-Frame-Options": "SAMEORIGIN" }`, and the `_admin_` root sets `"DENY"`.
13.2 [x] Expected (`dx/builder.md` `security`): the merge is outside-in, server then layouts then page, and a set key replaces. Actual: `/admin` answers `x-frame-options: SAMEORIGIN` on 200, 401, and 403. The root's `.headers()` (`x-docs-area: admin`) is applied on the same responses.
13.3 [x] Write a failing `server/security/apply.test.ts` or integration case where a root layout's `.security()` key beats the server's. Then collect the root and layout configs into the route config.
13.4 [x] Verify: `docs-server` `route security` sees `x-frame-options: DENY` on `/admin`.

14 [x] B14 Streaming server functions do not stream on the client
14.1 [x] Repro: `/playground`, then "Run build". `buildLog` is `createServerFn().input(…).stream(async function* …)`.
14.2 [x] Expected (`dx/server-fn.md`): the client function returns an async iterator of the yielded values. Actual: `pageerror: _(...)[Symbol.asyncIterator] is not a function`, then an unhandled `HTTP 200`. The log stays empty and the server keeps "running". The boundary transform strips the generator from the client chunk, so `record.stream` is `undefined` there, and `packages/core/src/client/boot/create-client.ts:145` routes the call to `callServerFn` (a JSON POST) instead of `streamServerFn`.
14.3 [x] Write a failing case (a client-graph stream function whose body was stripped is still called through `streamServerFn`). The kind must survive the strip, for example a `__stream: true` field the transform writes. Fix: the client graph keeps `.stream(undefined)`, so the record's `kind` comes from the method that ended the chain, and the client transport routes on it. Failing tests first: `builder.test.ts` `stream kind` and `create-client.test.ts` `stream call`.
14.4 [x] Verify: `docs-data` `stream` and `stream abort` are green on `node-prod-chromium`. `stream abort` waited for exactly two lines, which Playwright's 500 ms retry backoff skips past at one line per 150 ms. It now polls each frame for a second line, then checks that the count holds 500 ms after `aborted`. On node-prod-firefox it fails on B40.

15 [x] B15 `useSuspenseQuery` on a prerendered page stays on its fallback forever
15.1 [x] Repro: an SSG doc page (`/docs/auth`, prod) renders `<Feedback>`, which is `useSuspenseQuery(serverFnQueryOptions(feedbackCount, …))` under `<Loading>`.
15.2 [x] Expected: the stored shell holds the fallback, and the client runs the query after hydrate, or the prerender resolves it. Actual: `#feedback-loading` stays. No request to `feedbackCount` is ever sent, and no error is logged. This was seen with hydration otherwise working (B2 and B3 sidestepped).
15.3 [x] Write a failing integration case: a stored render with a pending query under `<Loading>`, hydrated, runs `queryFn` once. Then fix the hand-off.
15.4 [x] Verify: `docs-data` `mutation sets data` is green.
15.5 [x] Cause and fix: the stored body was cut at `</html>` (`server/cache/scrub.ts` `shellOf`), so the `<Loading>` fragment Solid flushes after the shell never reached the store, though the query itself was dehydrated as resolved. The client hydrated the boundary as pending and waited forever on its `_fr` promise, with no request because the cache already held the data. A stored write now reads to the end of Solid's render: a Flare `"stream"` hole read through `useDeferred` is a client source and holds nothing open, and app async is awaited. A render still open 10 s after its shell is served live, `private, no-store`, not stored, with one warn naming the route, and prerender fails that route (`write.test.ts` `render completes before store`, `stream defer stays a hole`, `incomplete render not stored`, `prerender incomplete fails`). The doc pages' feedback now renders. 15.4 still fails for another reason: one `feedbackCount` refetch after hydrate, TanStack refetching the stale hydrated query, which is 11.2's contract.
15.6 [x] Closed (flare-77's release-gate audit, 2026-10-15): fixed by 3357ca83 (a stored write waits for Solid's render to complete). `scrub.test.ts` `shell chunks` pins the cut, and docs-data `mutation sets data` passed on every project in the full local grid at 7405c053, 7b667393 and 9cf25d8a (1242 passed, 0 failed each, 19 projects) and in CI on main, green 14 runs in a row from 28048f4d through 9cf25d8a.

16 [x] B16 `InterceptOutlet` renders nothing on an intercepting navigation
16.1 [x] Repro: on `/`, type "route" in the header search and press Enter. Search is `.intercept({ from: ["[[locale]]/_root_/", …], render: "search-modal" })`, and the root renders `<InterceptOutlet>`.
16.2 [x] Expected (`dx/navigate.md` InterceptOutlet): the overlay opens over the mounted home page. Actual: `GET /search?q=route&_flare=data` succeeds, the URL becomes `/search?q=route`, and the home page stays, but the outlet child is never called: no `#search-modal`, no error. The generated record does carry `intercept: {"from":[…],"render":"search-modal"}`.
16.3 [x] Write a failing `shared/components/intercept-outlet.test.tsx` or e2e-level case where the `from` id is a root home page (`[[locale]]/_root_/`). Then fix the overlay open. Cause: the overlay opened and the child ran, but the search page's `Results` calls `useSearch` and `useParams` with the page's own id, and `MatchesContext` held only the background chain, so `matchOf` threw `is not a route on screen`. The throw had no boundary (B41), so Solid left the overlay's DOM detached and nothing reported it. Fix: `match.render` provides `MatchesContext` with the overlay's page match after the background chain. Failing test first: `intercept-outlet.test.tsx` `page hooks`.
16.4 [x] Verify: `docs-navigation` `search intercept` is green on node-prod-chromium, node-prod-firefox, and node-dev-chromium, and so is `docs-resilience` `overlay history`.

17 [x] B17 A client `rewrite` is not applied to anchor clicks
17.1 [x] Repro: the router has `rewrite(location)` mapping `/guide/*` to `/docs/*`. On `/examples`, click `<a href="/guide/routing">`.
17.2 [x] Expected (`dx/router.md` rewrite): the rewrite runs on the client before match, so the click is a client navigation to the routing doc. Actual: a full document load of `/guide/routing`, which the server answers 404. The anchor delegation checks for a route before it applies the rewrite. Leaving the page also logs `pageerror: Transition was skipped`, an unhandled view-transition rejection.
17.3 [x] Write a failing `client/navigation` case: a same-origin anchor whose href matches only after `rewrite` follows the client router. Separately, handle the skipped-transition rejection. Causes: `delegateClicks` checked the raw href against the routes before any rewrite, and its `visit` goes through `resolveUrl`, which never rewrites (it serves server-built URLs). The view transition's `ready` rejects with an AbortError when the transition is skipped, and nothing handled it. Fixes: `rewriteLocation` in `shared/router/create-router.ts` is the one rewrite step for `navigate`, `Link`, and anchor clicks, and the delegate gates the match on the rewritten pathname and visits the rewritten URL (d2b10df). `runTransition` marks `ready` handled. Failing tests first: `link.test.tsx` `delegate rewrite`, `direction.test.ts` `skipped transition`.
17.4 [x] Verify: `docs-routing` `client rewrite`, now with the empty-problems check, is green on node-prod-chromium and node-prod-firefox. On node-dev-chromium the navigation lands and the only problem is B7's font 404. `view transition scoped` and `view transition off` stay green on all three.

18 [x] B18 A `shallow` link runs the loader
18.1 [x] Repro: on `/examples?tab=cache`, click the `shallow` link that adds `note=shallow`.
18.2 [x] Expected (`dx/link.md`): `shallow` changes the URL and does not run loaders. Actual: `GET /examples?note=shallow&tab=cache&_flare=data` is sent and `#examples-request` changes (a new loader run).
18.3 [x] Cause: `shallow` was never implemented. Only `resolveTarget`'s different-pathname throw read it, so a shallow target was an ordinary data navigation. Fix (dx/navigate.md `shallow`): `resolveTarget` returns a `shallow` target for a same-page move that is not hash-only, and the navigator's `inPlace` step, shared with the hash move, asks the blocker, commits the URL with the page's search set to the call's validator output, writes history, and sends nothing. The cases live in `navigate-shallow.test.ts` (`navigate.test.ts` is at its line limit). Failing test first: `shallow`; `shallow blocked` passed before, since the blocker already ran.
18.4 [x] Verify: `docs-navigation` `shallow search` is green on node-prod-chromium, node-prod-firefox, and node-dev-chromium.

21 [x] B21 `withFetchDedupe` does not dedupe across loaders of one request
21.1 [x] Repro: the docs layout loader and the doc page loader both call `withFetchDedupe(serverCtx().upstream)("https://upstream.flare.test/stats")` (`apps/docs/src/stats.ts`). The upstream counts calls per request, and the page prints it.
21.2 [x] Expected (`dx/server.md` withFetchDedupe): the second identical GET in one request reuses the first, so `#fetch-calls` is `1`. Actual: `2`. Each loader runs under a child event (`server/loaders/scope.ts:26`, `Object.create(parent)`), and the first dedupe call inside a loader writes its store onto that child (`server/context/fetch-dedupe.ts` `reset` → `Reflect.set(event, STORE_KEY, …)`), so siblings never share it.
21.3 [x] Write a failing `server/context/fetch-dedupe.test.ts` case: two calls under two loader-scope children of one request event make one upstream call. Then keep the store on the root request event. Fix: `loaders/scope.ts` exports `requestEvent(event)`, which walks up from a loader or preloader child to the request's own event, and the dedupe store is read from and reset on it, so every loader, preloader, and the render share it. Failing tests first: `fetch-dedupe.test.ts` `loader siblings` (2 calls before), `loader reset` (a POST in a loader now resets the request's store; 2 calls before, 3 now).
21.4 [x] Verify: `docs-data` `fetch dedupe` is green 2 of 2 on node-prod-chromium, node-prod-firefox, and node-dev-chromium.

22 [x] B22 A segment's `cdn` default is not applied
22.1 [x] Repro: `(guide)/api/api.segment.ts` is `createPathSegment(…).cache({ cdn: { maxAge: "1m", swr: "10m", tags: ["api-reference"] } })`. Its page `api/[...path]` sets `cache: { ssr: { ttl: "10m", tags: ["api"] } }`. GET `/hr/api/server/create-server` twice (a tag in the URL, so no locale negotiation applies).
22.2 [x] Expected (`dx/cache.md` How the decision is made): `cdn` on a segment is a default for the pages under it, so the shared public hit sends `public, max-age=0, s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=600` and `Cache-Tag: api-reference`. Actual: `flare-cache: hit` with `cache-control: private, no-store` and no `Cache-Tag`. A page's own `cdn` (home on `/hr`) does send the public header.
22.3 [x] Write a failing `server/cacheability/decide.test.ts` case (`segment cdn` through the real chain, not a hand-built one). Then merge segment `cdn` into the chain. Root cause, wider than `cdn`: codegen never emitted a `*.segment.ts` file, so no segment ever joined a chain and none of its settings applied (types-gen listed it, the runtime never saw it). Fix: codegen puts a segment in `layouts` and `chunks` like a layout; `loadChain` gives a record with `_type: "segment"` the kind `"segment"`, which the boundary walk skips and the match cache keys as a layout; its `l` frame is `d: null`. Failing tests first: `routes.test.ts` `segment emitted`, `create-router.test.ts` `segment chain`; `decide.test.ts` `segment cdn served` (through `serveWith`) guards the runtime, which already merged a chained segment's `cdn`.

23 [x] B23 Locale negotiation overwrites a `.response()` page's Cache-Control
23.1 [x] Repro: `/llms.txt` is a `.response()` page under the `[[locale]]` root that returns `cache-control: public, max-age=300`.
23.2 [x] Expected (`dx/cache.md`): a `.response()` page is outside the page decision and keeps its own `Cache-Control` unless the chain reads auth. Actual: `/llms.txt` answers `private, no-store`, while `/hr/llms.txt` answers `public, max-age=300`. The "negotiated private" rule for documents with no tag (`dx/features.md` i18n) is being applied to a response route.
23.3 [x] Write a failing `features/i18n/server/middleware.test.ts` or integration case: a `.response()` route with no path tag keeps its Cache-Control. Then scope the rule to rendered documents. Cause: `match.ts` treated every `kind: "document"` request as a rendered document, and a `.response()` route's request is one, so it was marked `negotiated` (the downgrade) and 302ed on a cookie or Accept-Language that differed from the default. Fix (dx/features.md i18n): `localePage` carries the route's `response` flag; a response route is never negotiated, gets no locale cookie, and keeps its own Cache-Control, while a wrong-case tag still 301s and an unknown optional tag is still stripped. Failing tests first: `middleware.test.ts` `response route` (302 before), `response route tagged`.

28 [x] B28 CRITICAL: a client that disconnects mid-response kills the Node and Bun server
28.1 [x] Repro: build the docs app. Serve it with `createNodeListener(server)` under Node 24.21, or `createBunFetch(server)` under Bun 1.4.2. The scratch repro is `disconnect.mjs`: five requests per case, then a health check.
28.2 [x] Expected: a closed tab ends that response only. Actual: the process exits. `/healthz` and every later request get `ECONNREFUSED`. The log shows Solid's `RequestEvent is missing … non-managed async scope` twice, then the uncaught `Error: the query client getter runs inside a request on the server` (`features/query/client/getter.ts`). The render keeps running after the abort, outside the request scope, and the root's `getQueryClient()` throws with nothing to catch it. Workers survives, because an isolate error does not end the process. In the Playwright grid this is the `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` that `docs-resilience` `network failure` hits on node-prod and bun-prod, right after `missing chunk reload` closes a page while its reload document is still streaming.
28.3 [x] Write a failing `adapters/node/listener.test.ts` and `adapters/bun/serve.test.ts` case: a streaming document whose client aborts after the headers leaves the server answering. Stop the render when the request aborts, and never let a render-phase throw after an abort escape as an uncaught exception.

29 [x] B29 Workers formats numbers differently from Node, Bun, and the browser
29.1 [x] Repro: `/ar/search?q=r` on workers-prod. The footer is `formatMessage("{n, plural, one {# release} other {# releases}}", { n: 14 })` inside `LocaleProvider` (`ar`).
29.2 [x] Expected: the server and the client render the same text for the same tag (`dx/features.md` i18n formatMessage). Actual: the digits depend on the runtime's ICU. workerd and genuine Node 24.21 (ICU 78.3, CLDR 48.0: `new Intl.NumberFormat("ar").format(14)` is `14`) render `14 releases`. Bun 1.4.2 renders `١٤ releases` (Arabic-Indic digits), as the browsers do. A page's numbers change with the server runtime and can disagree with a client re-render.
29.3 [x] Decided: Flare pins no numbering system. Cause: CLDR 46 moved plain `ar` to Latin digits (`ar.xml` `defaultNumberingSystem` is `arab` through release-45 and inherits root's `latn` from release-46; `ar-EG` and `ar-SA` keep `arab`). Bun 1.4.2 bundled an ICU from before it, so it disagreed with Node's ICU 78.3. Today Node 24.21, Node 26.10, and Bun 1.4.3 (ICU 78.3), Chromium 149, Firefox 151, and workerd all format `ar` 14 as `14`. Digits are the runtime's CLDR data for the tag, as for every other `Intl` call on the page, and a `-u-nu-` tag fixes them everywhere (dx/features.md i18n). `format.test.ts` `numbering from tag` guards that contract; it passes without a source change. The e2e `format message` asserted the pre-CLDR-46 `١٤ releases`.
29.4 [x] Verify: `docs-features` `format message` now proves the provider's tag by the plural category (`/ar` `14 releases (many)`, `/` `14 releases`), red against the old footer, and is green on node-dev, node-prod, and workers-prod, chromium and firefox (6 of 6).

30.1 [x] Repro: on `/docs/routing`, let every `?_flare=data` response carry `flare-build: a-newer-deploy` (Playwright `route.fulfill` with the real body), then click a sidebar link. This was seen on bun-prod, workers-prod, and node-dev Firefox and WebKit, with hydration working (B2 and B3 sidestepped).
30.2 [x] Expected (`dx/navigate.md`): a navigation whose response `flare-build` differs from `self.flare.build` document-loads once under the `flare_chunk_reload` guard, and does not apply frames. Actual: no document request. The client applies the frames from the other build. With the real app, the case falsely passes, because dead clicks (B2, B3) fall through to a native page load.
30.3 [x] Write a failing `client/navigation/navigate.test.ts` case (`build mismatch`) through the real fetch path. Then compare the header on every data response.
30.4 [x] Verify: `docs-resilience` `build mismatch reload` is green with hydration working.
30.5 [x] Cause: the browser prefetcher was created with an empty build id, which turned its check off, so a prefetch a newer deploy answered was held and taken whole by the next navigation with no request of its own. One id now feeds the navigator, the prefetcher, and the server-function transport. The spec also simulated the deploy on `_flare=data` only and after load; a real deploy stamps prefetches too, so it now marks both from the start.

31 [x] B31 Concurrent misses of one shared key render once each
31.1 [x] Repro: on a production build, send 8 parallel GETs to a never-requested shared SSR URL, `/hr/api/routes/create-page`, and read `#api-rendered-by` from each body.
31.2 [x] Expected: one render fills the store and the others wait for it (a single-flight miss). `dx/cache.md` names an in-flight slot only for the stale refresh, so decide whether misses join it. Actual: 8 distinct request ids, so 8 renders, on node-prod (Node adapter on Bun) and bun-prod. One scratch run on node-prod rendered once, so the result depends on timing. Without coalescing, a cold popular page makes every concurrent visitor pay for a render.
31.3 [x] Cause (B31 and B33): misses already shared the in-flight slot, but it freed when the render settled, and the store write ran later in `waitUntil`. Logged: 8 parallel misses reached `inflight` 20 to 110 ms apart, each after the previous flight had freed its slot and before its write landed, so each read a miss and rendered. Fix (dx/cache.md "What gets stored"): the slot spans the render and its write, without delaying the first response, and a flight re-reads the store before rendering, serving an entry written since as a hit. A failed render or write frees the slot. Failing tests first: `read.test.ts` `concurrent miss`, `miss after render`; `slot frees after write` guards the release; `inflight.test.ts` `coalesce` covers the held slot.
31.4 [x] Verify: 8 parallel cold misses now share one `api-rendered-by` id (3 urls, 2 runs, node-prod), and `docs-resilience` `concurrent misses render once` passes 3 of 3 on node-prod-chromium, bun-prod-chromium, and workers-prod-chromium.

34 [x] B34 Playwright's Firefox driver never reports a page's main-world context, so a wait on it stalls
34.1 [x] Repro: the retired `i18n.test.ts` `theme no flash` on node-prod-firefox at `746b8d1`, genuine Node 24.21. With tracing off, 0 of 120 runs failed locally, and CI run 38362077182 failed once (1 failed, 157 passed). With `--trace retain-on-failure`, 1 to 2 runs in 100 fail (1/40, 2/40, 2/100, 4/500). The failing step is `flarePage(page).load` on the second page of a context that has `context.addInitScript`: `waitForSelector("html[data-flare-hydrated]")` runs out the 30s timeout.
34.2 [x] The framework is right, and Juggler (Playwright's Firefox driver) is at fault. During the stall, a screenshot of that page shows `#theme` as `dark`, which only the hydrated client writes (the server renders `system`). A live Gecko profile shows the page's content process idle in its event loop. The first page still answers `evaluate`, and a fresh third page in the same context hydrates. The `pw:protocol` log of 400 sessions has one outlier. After the second page's navigation destroys its contexts, Juggler emits `Runtime.executionContextCreated` for `__playwright_utility_world__` only, never the main world, then `navigationCommitted`, `load`, and `sameDocumentNavigation`. Playwright waits for a main-world context that never comes, so it never sends the evaluate. Closing the first page before opening the second does not change the rate (4/500).
34.3 [x] Separate spec race found on the way: `tabs sync` started `nextStorageEvent(first)` without awaiting its listener before `second` wrote `localStorage`, so the event could fire first (31 of 80 runs with tracing on, 2 of 20 off). With the listener registered and awaited first, 40 of 40 passed. The docs spec now registers it first.
34.4 [x] After phase 6 (hydration), compare 500 traced runs of the case seeding storage with `context.addInitScript` against 500 seeding it with a `storageState` context. If `addInitScript` is the trigger, the spec seeds through `storageState`, and a Playwright issue is filed with the protocol excerpt. Measured 2026-10-14, node-prod-firefox, Playwright 1.61.1, Firefox 151, 1 worker, both seeds interleaved in one file, 52.3 min: `addInitScript` 499 passed and 1 timed out, `storageState` 497 passed and 3 timed out. Every failure's only unfinished action is `waitForSelector("html[data-flare-hydrated]")` on the context's second page. `addInitScript` is not the trigger, so the spec stays. Closed as upstream (Playwright's Firefox driver, Juggler's main-world context loss, 34.2); an issue is drafted, not filed. Traces: outside the repo, `~/.cache/flare-gates/b34/test-results` (repeats 83, 91, 253, 448).
34.5 [x] Not a fix; rate measured: about 1 in 250 traced runs of `theme no flash` on node-prod-firefox stall in Playwright's driver (34.4), whichever way storage is seeded.
34.6 [x] CI 38594334539, e2e-linux 1/3: node-prod-firefox `navigation.test.ts` `scroll` failed with `page.evaluate: Execution context was destroyed` at its first evaluate after `open("/")` had hydrated. Not reproduced locally: the whole spec ×8 (248 passed) and `scroll` ×40 on node-prod-firefox (flare-77). It fits this entry's shape, Juggler losing the main-world context, more than a real navigation, but CI kept no evidence to tell them apart: tracing is off. 36 [x] B36 Back after two client navigations shows the page being left
36.1 [x] Repro: node-prod or node-dev, Chromium. `/`, click Routing (`#doc-title` "Routing"), click Caching in the sidebar ("Caching", `history.state` `{ index: 2 }`), then `page.goBack()`. The URL becomes `/docs/routing` (`{ index: 1 }`), and the client sends `GET /docs/routing?_flare=data`, which answers the routing doc's frames. `#doc-title` stays "Caching". This is `docs-smoke` `click through` on every project, and the same shape as B32's `scroll restoration`.
36.2 [x] Cause, with evidence: the navigator is right. The pop step lands, it is still the latest navigation, `settle` builds the routing snapshot, and `store.commit(next)` then `flush()` run. Directly after them, `store.snapshot()` still returns `/docs/caching`: Solid 2 holds the write in a transition that the new tree's async keeps pending, so the old page stays with no fallback. The pending async is the doc page's `<Feedback>`, `useSuspenseQuery(serverFnQueryOptions(feedbackCount, …))`. On these prerendered pages it never settles (B15). With `<Feedback>` removed (a local experiment, reverted), Back lands at once. The same pop through the unit fixture's navigator on a page with no async lands, and reduced motion (no view transition) changes nothing.
36.3 [x] Root cause (no longer blocked on B15): on a quick forward visit, solid-query starts the doc page's suspense query's first fetch inside the committing component's render, where an owner exists and no observer runs. The client stub's render guard (B45) threw `feedbackCount was called during a render` there, so no request was sent, TanStack counted one failure, and its 1 s, 2 s, and 4 s retry backoff kept the query `pending, fetching`. Back rendered that page again into the pending query, and Solid's transition held the store write (Solid's dev build reports it as `[SILENT_HOLD]`). With a pause on the page, the retry ran from the retryer's timer outside any owner and succeeded, which is why the failure depended on timing. Fix (B45 as revised in ff82e5b): the render check warns in dev through `devWarn` and never throws, and `serverFnQueryOptions` runs its call outside any owner. Failing tests first: `client-stub.test.tsx` `client stub` and `on settled`, and `options.test.ts` `queryFn inside a render`.
36.4 [x] Verify: `docs-smoke` `click through` is green on node-prod-chromium and node-prod-firefox, and `docs-navigation` `scroll restoration` is green on node-prod-chromium. On node-dev-chromium both Back steps land, and the spec's empty-console check still fails, only on B7's font 404 and B39's two diagnostics.

40 [x] B40 Stopping a client stream does not abort its request
40.1 [x] Repro: node-prod-firefox, `/playground`, "Run build", then "Stop" after the second line. `docs-data` `stream abort` waits for `aborted` and reads `running`.
40.2 [x] Expected (`dx/server-fn.md`): stopping the iteration, by `break`, `return()`, or a thrown consumer, aborts the request at once, and a pending `next()` resolves done. Actual: `streamServerFn` is an async generator and `send` passes no signal to `fetch`. `return()` queues behind the pending `next()` until the next line lands, then only cancels the response body. Chromium turns that cancel into a network abort (`ERR_ABORTED` about 150 ms after Stop). Firefox did not: the playground's `buildStatus` GET finished at 1284 ms, before the stream request failed at 1317 ms (`NS_BASE_STREAM_CLOSED`), so the server still said `running`. A stream that yields rarely cannot be stopped until its next value, in any engine.
40.3 [x] Fix (`bits/07` `stop aborts`): `streamServerFn` owns an `AbortController` and `send` takes its signal. The client function's iterator aborts on `return()` at once, and a pending `next()` resolves `{ done: true }` with no rejection: the stop races the reader instead of queuing behind it. Failing test first: `client/server-fn/stream.test.ts` `stop aborts`.
40.4 [x] Verify: `docs-data` `stream abort` is green on node-prod-firefox, node-prod-chromium, and node-dev-chromium, twice each, and `stream` and `stream fill` stay green.

41 [x] B41 A throw while `InterceptOutlet` renders its child vanishes
41.1 [x] Repro: an overlay page whose render throws, such as B16's `matchOf` throw, on the client. Nothing paints, nothing reaches the console or the dev overlay, and the overlay's DOM stays detached.
41.2 [x] Expected (`dx/navigate.md` InterceptOutlet): the child renders inside the same boundary walk as `Outlet`, so a throw in the child or the page paints the nearest `errorRender` of the layout that owns the outlet (or the built-in div) and sends the dev overlay payload once. Actual: `Outlet`'s slots render under the B77 walk (`Errored` plus `errorRender`), and `InterceptOutlet`'s child does not.
41.3 [x] Fix (`bits/06` `layout hook throws`, `child throw reported`): each overlay's child renders inside `ownerGuard`, `Outlet`'s `Errored` guard with the walk starting at the layout that owns the outlet, so the failure paints in the outlet and `onBoundaryError` runs once. Failing tests first: before the fix, the throw escaped to the root's guard and replaced the whole root in jsdom.
41.4 [x] Verify: `docs-navigation` `search intercept` and `docs-resilience` `overlay history` stay green on node-prod-chromium, node-prod-firefox, and node-dev-chromium. In dev chromium, with B16's provider taken out locally, the overlay's throw painted the root's `errorRender` (`#root-error`) in the outlet while the home page stayed mounted.

42 [x] B42 A client-side error never opens the dev overlay, and an SSR error opens it in every tab
42.1 [x] Repro: `vite dev`, a client navigation whose render throws inside a boundary (B41's case) or applies an `e` frame. The boundary paints, and `<vite-error-overlay>` never opens. Separately, an SSR error from one tab's request opens the overlay in every connected tab (B38).
42.2 [x] Expected (`dx/plugin.md`, B93): a payload opens the overlay only in the tab whose request or render caused it. Actual: the client's `devSend` calls `import.meta.hot.send("flare:error", payload)`, which goes to the server, and `vite/dev/handler.ts` relays only the ssr environment's `flare:error`, to `server.ws.send`, a broadcast. Nothing listens for the client's event.
42.3 [x] Fix (`dx/plugin.md` Development errors, B93, B40's one exception): the dev handler answers a client's `flare:error` to that client (f10312e). For a request with no `flare-dev-tab` header it mints a token, hands it to the app as that header, and writes `<meta name="flare-dev-tab">` into the page after the app answered, so no store keeps it. The tab announces it with `flare:tab` and names it on its own requests. A request's dev sink tags its payload with the token, and the hub (`vite/dev/tabs.ts`) sends it to that tab only, holding one for a tab not yet announced for 30 s and 20 payloads. A production server has no dev sink and never reads the header. Failing tests first: handler.test `client error relayed`, `other tab silent`, `document error after connect`; create-client.test `dev tab`; answers.test `prod tab header`; no-flare-header-branching.test `dev tab header`.
42.4 [x] Verify: a two-tab dev chromium probe: a document whose loader throws, a client navigation to it, and a client render throw (B16's provider removed locally) each open the overlay in the causing tab and not the other. `docs-data` `defer rejects`, `stream fill`, `stream`, and `stream abort` are green 4 of 4 on node-dev-chromium. A full node-dev-chromium run fails only on known bugs (B7's font 404, B39's diagnostics, B21, B22, B23, B29).

43 [x] B43 The progress bar never shows pending on Firefox
43.1 [x] Repro: `docs-navigation` `progress bar` on node-prod-firefox. With every `?_flare=data` request delayed 700 ms, a sidebar click from `/docs/routing` to Auth never sets `#progress` `data-pending="true"`; the spec times out at that check. It fails 4 of 4 runs after afeec67 and 2 of 2 on a fresh worktree at 7bd1b9f, and passes 4 of 4 on node-prod-chromium.
43.2 [x] Cause, a spec race, not a framework bug: the click's hover starts an intent prefetch (`/docs/auth?_flare=prefetch`), and the navigation takes it over, so no `?_flare=data` request is ever made and the spec's 700 ms delay on data requests never applies. Pending then lasts 10 to 15 ms (probe: chromium 43 to 53 ms, firefox 127 to 142 ms after the click), which the expect poll usually misses, and Firefox's slower click lets the prefetch start first every time. Fix: the spec delays prefetch requests as well as data requests.
43.3 [x] Verify: `docs-navigation` `progress bar` is green 3 of 3 on node-prod-firefox, node-prod-chromium, and node-dev-chromium.

46 [x] B46 Under the Bun runtime, the fs store's default directory lands in the repo
46.1 [x] Repro (from B27.5, Linux, Bun 1.4.3): `bun --bun run --filter @lovrozagar/flare test`. `adapters/stores/fs/store.test.ts` `dir` fails and leaves `packages/core/.flare/cache` in the repo. Under Node 24 it passes. Re-measure on the current tree first. Plan leaf 8.17.
46.2 [x] Cause: `createFilesystemStore` resolved its default with `resolve(".flare/cache")`. Node's `path.resolve` calls `process.cwd()`, Bun's reads the native cwd, so the test's cwd mock was ignored under Bun and the store wrote into the package. Fix: `resolve(process.cwd(), ...)`. Failing case first: `store.test.ts` `dir` also asserts nothing was written under the real cwd (red under Bun 1.4.3). Verify: `dir` passes under Node and under the Bun runtime, and the Bun run leaves no `.flare` directory.

47 [x] B47 Under the Bun runtime, the font export cases fail on a URL scheme
47.1 [x] Repro (from B27.5): the same Bun run fails `exports.test.ts` `font subpath` and `font barrel` with "The URL must be of scheme file". Under Node 24 they pass. Re-measure on the current tree first. Plan leaf 8.18.
47.2 [x] Cause: the test's stage resolver runs `process.execPath`, which under `--bun` is Bun, and Bun's `import.meta.resolve` answers differently: an unexported subpath (`./fonts`) is ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND, not Node's ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED, and a pattern id with no file is ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND, where Node returns the URL of the absent file. Real ids resolve the same on both. Fix: the package is unchanged; `exports.test.ts` keeps the runtime's own resolver and names each runtime's answer. Verify: `font subpath` and `font barrel` pass under Node and under the Bun runtime.

51 [x] B51 A project root behind a symlink skips the boundary transform for every app module
51.1 [x] Repro: macOS CI run 38486702944, `bun run test` under Node 24: the fixtures' roots sit in `os.tmpdir()`, `/var/folders/...`, a symlink to `/private/var`. Tripwire's 6 cases, environments `order`, `public once`, `urls agree`, `chunk urls`, flare `hmr keeps split`, and inject `reset on` fail. The same 12 fail on Linux with `TMPDIR` set to a symlink.
51.2 [x] Cause: Vite gives module ids as real paths, while `config.root` is only `resolve(root)`. `flare.ts` `appModule` took `relative(root, id)`, so with a root reached through a symlink every app module looked outside the app: the boundary transform skipped them, and the B45 tripwire failed the build on the bodies left in. The client entry's manifest key and inject's reset check missed the same way, and Vite's dev server refused a real-path module under a symlinked root.
51.3 [x] Fix: `vite/plugin/real-path.ts` `realPath`. `flare()` takes the root's real path and returns it as Vite's `root`, the boundary plugin checks a module against the real and the configured root, and inject matches the client entry by either. Failing tests first: `tripwire.test.ts` and `environments.test.ts` `symlinked root` (red with the old plugin), `real-path.test.ts` `real path`.
51.4 [x] Verify: the four files pass 37 of 37 with `TMPDIR` plain and set to a symlink; e2e-mac's next run closes the macOS side.

52 [x] B52 The hydration mount refetches every query the document filled
52.1 [x] Repro: CI run 38490356179, e2e-mac, node-dev-webkit `docs-smoke` `/docs/routing`: `[SILENT_HOLD] writes to "signal" (3 → 4) were held 349ms` in `<Tally>`. Locally, a document load of `/docs/routing` in docs dev sends `GET /_flare/fn/…?_i={"slug":"routing"}` after hydrate, though the server filled that query into the document's `queries`.
52.2 [x] Cause: `features/query/client/getter.ts` seeds the client from the document but keeps TanStack's default `staleTime` 0, so the seeded query is stale on its first mount and `refetchOnMount` runs it again. On a slow runner the refetch holds solid-query's result write past Solid's 200 ms `SILENT_HOLD` warning. The search page hid it with `staleTime: 60_000`, and the `client seed` unit case checks the seed, not a mount.
52.3 [x] Fix (B102): the seeded client's default `refetchOnMount` answers `false` for a document-filled query until the hydrated phase, and TanStack's default after. Failing tests first: getter.test `hydration mount`, `hydration mount app wins`, `hydration mount not in document`; the search page drops its `staleTime` so `docs-data` `query prefetched` is red; new `docs-data` `query hydrates once`. Done: `getter.ts` seeds a `QueryClient` whose default `refetchOnMount` is `phase.hydrated || !filled.has(query.queryHash)`, and the client boot calls the registered getter's `hydrated()` right after `ready.hydrated()` (`shared/protocol` `QueryGetter.hydrated`). The ordering is proven, not assumed: the unit cases call `hydrated()` right after `render` returns with no flush in between, as `bootClient` does after `mountApp`, and the query function still runs 0 times, so solid-query evaluates `refetchOnMount` during the mount itself.
52.4 [x] Verify: the three unit cases and both `docs-data` cases pass on every project, and e2e-mac's next run is green. Local: the three getter cases pass; `query prefetched` (with the search page's `staleTime` removed) and `query hydrates once` were red with the old getter (the extra `/_flare/fn/` GET) and pass 12 of 12 on node-dev and node-prod chromium, 3 runs each. `query hydrates once` counts from the document load: the refetch went out during the hydration mount, before `data-flare-hydrated`. e2e-mac's next run is the remaining check.

53 [x] B53 The hydrate harness fails when another process holds Vite's HMR port
53.1 [x] Repro: `tests/integration/hydrate.test.tsx` failed 6 of 12 (chromium and firefox) with `pageerror: WebSocket closed without opened.` on 2026-10-14 while an unrelated process held `*:24678`. The same commits pass 12 of 12 in fresh worktrees with the port free. An accept-and-destroy TCP server on 24678 (`.tmp/grunt/p14/port-squat.mjs`) reproduces it on demand: 5 of 12.
53.2 [x] Cause: the harness's Vite runs in middleware mode with `hmr: false`, but Flare's client modules read `import.meta.hot`, so import analysis still injects `/@vite/client`, which dials Vite's default HMR port. Any listener there that closes the socket raises a pageerror in each case still collecting problems. `ws: false` does not stop the dial.
53.3 [x] Fix: `hydrate.harness.ts` creates its HTTP server first and passes `server: { middlewareMode: true, hmr: { server }, watch: null }`, so the client's socket goes to the page's own origin, which Vite answers. `watch: null` keeps file events off. Failing test first: hydrate.test `foreign hmr port`.
53.4 [x] Verify: `foreign hmr port` red with the old harness and green with the fix; the full hydrate.test 12 of 12 with 24678 squatted and with it free. Measured: `foreign hmr port` failed 2 of 2 alone with the old harness and passes 2 of 2 with the fix (one tree alone could finish before the socket closed, so the case hydrates three). The full hydrate.test passes 13 of 13 in 6 runs with 24678 held by an accept-and-close server and in 3 runs with the port free. No product dev path runs Vite in middleware mode: flare() never sets it, and `vite/dev/handler.ts` installs nothing when an app does, so that app owns its HMR server.

54 [x] B54 On Workers, a query a loader prefetched never reaches the document's `queries`
54.1 [x] Repro: CI run 38493113436, e2e-linux workers-prod-chromium `docs-data` `query prefetched`: after the load the client sends `GET /_flare/fn/2bb6a87ae8e468cc?_i={"q":"route"}`. Locally it fails on workers-prod-chromium and passes on node-prod and bun-prod. A probe of `/search?q=route` shows the Workers document's `queries` empty (Node's hold `["search","route"]`), and the request leaves during the hydration mount (106 ms against `data-flare-hydrated` at 121 ms): nothing was seeded. At c439109f the Workers `queries` were empty too and no request went out: solid-query's own server serializer (`QueryClientProvider` → `serializeCacheOnServer`, an `sq:["search","route"]` hydration key, present on both runtimes) primed the client, and the search page's `staleTime: 60_000` kept that copy fresh, so the empty `queries` stayed hidden until B52 removed the staleTime.
54.2 [x] Cause: on the server, solid-query's `QueryClientProvider` registers `onCleanup(() => { client.cancelQueries(); client.clear(); })` on the request's client. A probe of `getter.ts` under workerd shows the loader and the render share the request event's `locals` and one client, the query added, fetched, and `success`, then `removed`, and only then `dehydrate()` with no queries: on Workers that cleanup runs before the document state is written, while on Node the state is written first. The request event and `locals` identity are not the cause.
54.3 [x] Fix: the request client keeps each query that succeeds, through a cache subscription made when the client is created, and `dehydrate()` adds the kept queries a clear removed to the live cache's. Failing test first: `getter.ssr.test.ts` `cleared before dehydrate` (a prefetch, a failed prefetch, then `clear()`: the state still holds the successful one), red before the fix. A workerd-level case would need Solid's SSR compile and the provider inside a bun-built worker, which `tests/workerd` does not do; the getter case pins the cause on every runtime.
54.4 [x] Verify: `docs-data` `query prefetched` was red on workers-prod-chromium with the old getter, and `query prefetched` and `query hydrates once` pass 18 of 18 on node-prod, bun-prod, and workers-prod chromium, 3 runs each.

55 [x] B55 `stream abort` raced its own stop against the end of the stream
55.1 [x] Repro: CI run 38493113436, e2e-mac node-prod-webkit `docs-data` `stream abort`: `#stream-status` read `running` twice, then `done`, never `aborted`. The same case passed in run 38490356179's e2e-mac, so it is intermittent. Locally it passes 20 of 20 on node-prod-webkit (the pinned container's WebKit).
55.2 [x] Cause: timing in the test, not a lost abort. The build log streams 6 lines at 150 ms, about 900 ms, and the test clicks Stop after the second line. Locally, in-page timestamps put the second line at about 305 ms and the click at about 350 ms on WebKit and Chromium (30 of 30 runs `aborted`), so a runner that delays the click by about 550 ms more lets the stream finish first. Clicking Stop 1 s after the second line reproduces CI's `done` exactly on node-prod-webkit and node-prod-chromium (2 of 2 each). CI kept no trace to read the click time from.
55.3 [x] Fix: the docs build log takes `hold`; a held run waits after its second line until the stop arrives, at most 10 s, and the card passes it when the playground URL has `?hold`. `stream abort` opens `/playground?hold`, so a late click still meets a running stream, while a stop that never reached the server would still end `done` and fail. The playground without `?hold` is unchanged.
55.4 [x] Verify: a Stop 1 s late gives `aborted` with `?hold` and `done` without it (node-prod webkit and chromium, 2 of 2 each); `stream abort`, `stream`, and `stream fill` pass 24 of 24 on node-prod chromium and firefox, node-dev chromium, and bun-prod chromium (3 runs each), and `stream abort` 10 of 10 on node-prod-webkit. e2e-mac's next runs are the macOS check.

56 [x] B56 `csrf headers` failed on workers-prod when a POST followed a CSRF 403 on the same connection
56.1 [x] Repro: CI's workers-prod `csrf headers` failed intermittently. A direct probe (.tmp/grunt/p14/b56-keepalive.ts: a docs prod boot, 3 POSTs to the echo fn on one `http.Agent({ keepAlive: true, maxSockets: 1 })`) gives "403, ECONNRESET, 200" in 200 of 200 rounds on workers-prod, and "403 403 200" on node-prod (200 of 200) and bun-prod (100 of 100). Playwright's request context only sometimes reuses the socket, so the case passed 50 of 50 locally.
56.2 [x] Cause: local Miniflare resets a kept-alive connection when the worker answers without reading the request body, and the CSRF check answers 403 before reading it. Cancelling the unread body in the adapter still resets (100 of 100). Draining it fixes the reset (100 of 100).
56.3 [x] Ruling: no adapter change. At Cloudflare the client talks to the edge, not to Miniflare's Node front, and draining up to 64 KiB on every early answer spends CPU and delays it for a simulator artifact. Plan 13.11 checks the real edge; if it resets too, the drain is reconsidered.
56.4 [x] Fix: `csrf headers` sends the rejected POSTs without a body, so nothing is left unread, and the same-origin POST keeps its body. The ruling's `connection: close` was measured first and does not reliably avoid the reset: with that header on a kept-alive Node agent, the probe still gave "403, ECONNRESET, 200" in 100 of 100 rounds. A fresh connection per POST and bodiless rejected POSTs both gave "403 403 403 200" in 50 of 50. dx/adapters.md records the behavior beside "Client disconnect on Workers".
56.5 [x] Canary: workerd/disconnect.test `miniflare resets unread body` asserts ["403", "ECONNRESET"] for two POSTs on one kept-alive socket to a worker that answers 403 unread. A worker that drains the body turns it red (["403", "403"]), which is the cue to give the rejected POSTs their body back.
56.6 [x] Verify: `csrf headers` passes 15 of 15 on node-dev, node-prod, bun-prod, and workers-prod chromium and node-prod-firefox (3 runs each).
57 [x] B57 In production, dark mode rendered the header and streamed content light
57.1 [x] Repro: the docs app in prod with the dark theme: the header computed `bg-stone-50/85` (oklab L 0.985) instead of the dark `bg-ink-950/85` (L 0.152), and a streamed changelog card kept the light `border-stone-200`. Dev was right. Measured on node-prod-chromium with HEAD's sources: header `oklab(0.985 … / 0.85)`, card border `oklch(0.923 0.003 48.717)`; dev: `oklab(0.152091 … / 0.85)` and `rgb(28, 28, 39)`.
57.2 [x] Cause, two faults. (1) `#flare-critical` came after the stylesheet link, so its copy of a light rule won the tie with the sheet's `dark:` rule of equal specificity in the same layer, even after the sheet loaded. A rule only streamed content uses has no copy in the critical style, so the card's base border won over its `dark:` border. (2) The document never passed the theme and direction attributes to the extractor (`withCritical` got no `ctx`), so `present` was empty and every `dark:` rule was dropped from the critical style. The matcher itself was right: with the attributes passed, it keeps `&:where([data-theme="dark"], [data-theme="dark"] *)` for shell elements.
57.3 [x] Fix (B103): in a document render, `<Stylesheet />` renders nothing in place and leaves its href in the request's placement (`placeStylesheet`, beside the nonce slot, so the hydrated tree is unchanged). `withCritical` writes the stylesheet markup right after `#flare-critical`, or alone before `</head>` when no rule matched. The document passes `criticalContext(router)`: the router's theme attribute and direction attribute, and `dir`, since the direction script sets both. Outside a document render the component still writes its markup in place.
57.4 [x] Tests: critical-style.test `insert`, `no css`, and `present variants`; document.test `critical shell` (red on HEAD: the critical style ended the head and had no dark rule); stylesheet.test `placed`; docs-features `stylesheet after critical` (red on HEAD on node-prod, bun-prod, and workers-prod chromium and node-prod-firefox: `#flare-critical` came after the preload link).
57.5 [x] Verify: with the fix, prod's header and card compute exactly dev's colors in dark mode, and `stylesheet after critical`, `tailwind critical`, `css module`, and `reset` pass 48 of 48 (3 runs on those four projects).
58 [x] B58 A superseded navigation threw an unhandled AbortError
58.1 [x] Repro: clicking links quickly logged "Uncaught (in promise) AbortError: signal is aborted without reason" from navigate.ts (`latest?.abort()`) via link.tsx, in dev and prod. That stack is the aborter's (the new click), not the reader's. docs-smoke `rapid links` reproduces it on every runtime: with HEAD's frames.ts it fails 10 of 10 on node-dev, node-prod, bun-prod, and workers-prod chromium and node-prod-firefox, each with "AbortError: BodyStreamBuffer was aborted" pageerrors.
58.2 [x] Cause: landFrames returns at `r` and leaves `settleLater` reading the deferred frames as a floating promise. A newer navigation aborts the request, the browser errors the body with an AbortError, `frames.next()` rejects, and nothing catches it. A body that breaks before `r` was already caught: `arrive` settles the request with `allSettled`.
58.3 [x] A first fix rejected the still-pending values with the abort reason. That is wrong: the old page stays on screen until the new navigation commits, so a value it shows failed into the page's error boundary (`page-error` in the unit case), and the dev overlay reported "BodyStreamBuffer was aborted". The full e2e run caught it on node-dev chromium and firefox, where the page stayed on Changelog behind the overlay.
58.4 [x] Fix: `settleLater` catches a broken body. When the request's signal aborted (LandOptions `signal`, which the navigation's request passes), a newer navigation owns the screen, so the pending values stay pending. Any other break (a dropped connection) rejects each one with its reason, so none waits forever. Prefetch passes no signal and never aborts.
58.5 [x] Tests: navigate-slots.test `superseded resolves` lands two deferred values, aborts the body with a second navigation whose response is held, checks the first page still shows both fallbacks, then lets the second commit, stops a third with a blocker, and records `unhandledRejection`. It is red with HEAD's frames.ts (the rejection) and with the first fix (`root[page-error]`). frames.test `broken body` covers both branches. navigate.test sits near the 500-line limit, so the case lives with the deferred-slot cases. `rapid links` starts from Changelog, whose 900 ms `stats` value is still loading at the second click (asserted), because the playground's 120 ms tip had already resolved on a loaded dev server.
58.6 [x] Verify: `rapid links` passes 15 of 15 (3 runs each on the five projects above), all docs specs pass 699 of 699 on node-dev chromium and firefox (3 runs, where the first fix failed 2), and the navigation unit tests pass.

59 [x] B59 A dev hard load showed the page unstyled until the client modules ran
59.1 [x] Report: in dev, a hard reload of a docs page showed unstyled content for a moment. The dev document carried no CSS: Vite's client injected each style when the client modules ran, and dx/head.md said "Dev renders nothing, since Vite injects the CSS".
59.2 [x] Fix (B104): the server writes one `<style data-vite-dev-id>` per CSS module of the client entry (flare's reset) and the matched route chain, in import order, in `<Stylesheet />`'s place, with the request's nonce, so the first paint is styled. The server asks the dev server over the server environment's hot channel (`flare:styles` with the chain's route ids; the ssr runner's channel on Node and Bun, workerd's in Workers dev). The dev server maps the ids to route files with the route codegen's scan, walks that environment's module graph, reads each CSS module as Vite serves it with `?direct` (Tailwind's entry comes out compiled), and caches the answer per chain until any watched file changes. A document that gets no answer within 2 s renders without dev styles and logs one dev warning. Vite's client seeds its style map from `style[data-vite-dev-id]`, so it adopts the server's styles and HMR keeps one per module. The reset's resolved id lost its NUL prefix (`virtual:flare/reset.css`), because an HTML parser turns a NUL in an attribute into U+FFFD and the client would have added a second reset; its loader also accepts `?direct`.
59.3 [x] Tests: vite/dev/dev-styles.test (`chain styles`, `cache`, `bad request`), server/render/dev-styles.test (`round trip`, `bounded wait`), shared/protocol/dev-styles.test, projector `dev styles sink`, stylesheet `dev`, dev-document `dev styles` (root first, a page without a CSS module gets only the root's), and docs-smoke `dev first paint` (every script blocked: body margin 0, h1 600, header sticky at DOMContentLoaded) and `dev styles adopted` (one style per id after hydration and after an HMR edit of styles.css, Tailwind compiled). `dev first paint` is red at HEAD on node-dev and bun-dev chromium (margin 8px); `dev styles adopted` passes at HEAD too, since the client injects its own styles, so it guards the adoption, not the bug.
59.4 [x] Verify: both e2e cases pass 12 of 12 on node-dev chromium and firefox and bun-dev chromium (2 runs). Workers dev writes the same three styles (that answer came from Node, BUGS.md 76; in workerd both cases pass on workers-dev chromium and firefox). Dev document time, docs app, fresh worktrees, 3 boots each: the warm document median 26.0 to 26.6 ms on Node against 26.0 to 26.2 before, and 25.0 to 26.9 on Bun against 24.8 to 25.4; the first document after boot 30 ms slower on each (the first walk and reads).
