61 [x] B61 Firefox flickered darker and lighter on card navigation in the docs app
61.1 [x] Report: in the user's own Firefox 155, the main content flickered "darker then lighter" on client navigation, "maybe what's in the view transition boundary". Two earlier readings of it were wrong. A doc page drawn at the old scroll offset was a probe artifact: Playwright's emulated viewport was taller than the tiled window, so Firefox scrolled an outer container (with no emulation, an emulated viewport that fits, or the system Firefox 155, the paint was right on every visit). A content shift after sidebar navigation did not reproduce: nothing moved per frame with overlay or classic scrollbars, in Playwright's Firefox 151 or the system Firefox 155.
61.2 [x] Measure: the mean luminance of the `#article` screen region per frame, recorded at 75 fps on the window's rectangle (.tmp/grunt/p14/b61/record-window.sh, the window found by a B61 title prefix), in the system Firefox 155.0.1 and Chrome 154 with throwaway profiles, driven by a script a local proxy injects (sys-browser.ts). The docs app in dev, dark theme. Flicker shows as direction flips (larger than 1.5) inside a transition, with frames at the page background. Home-card navigations, 4 each: Firefox flips 7, 9, 7, 7 (for example `17.9 13.6 13.3 19.5 12.2 21.3 12.0 11.9`); Chrome 0 on all 4. Sidebar navigations did not flicker in either browser.
61.3 [x] Cause: a Firefox bug that the docs app's content triggers. A home-card navigation is a document transition in both browsers, and the guide layout's `#article` (named `view-transition-name: article`) enters it with no old image while doc.page's `animate-rise` runs inside it. Firefox drops frames to the page background while content inside an entering named element animates. Toggled one at a time in Firefox: without the name, flips 0 to 1; without `animate-rise`, 0; with view transitions off, 0. A minimal page outside Flare (min2.ts: a named article whose child runs the same keyframes) drops frames on 4 of 4 entrances in Firefox and 0 of 3 in Chrome, and 0 of 4 in Firefox without the name or without the animation, so neither Flare's `skipTransition` handling nor its default `types` is involved. Firefox computes `mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter` on the images as the spec says. A transform-only rise still drops frames (flips 1 to 5).
61.4 [x] Fix (docs app, no framework change): doc.page has no entrance animation. The view transition is the entrance. Rejected: `:root:active-view-transition .animate-rise { animation: none }` restarts the animation from opacity 0 when the transition ends, a worse dip in both browsers. The search modal keeps `animate-rise`, since it renders in the root layout outside any named element. dx/navigate.md gains the rule and the supported pattern: animate an entrance on `::view-transition-new(name):only-child`, which measured smooth in Firefox 155 (4 of 4 card entrances, flips 0), and the note that a browser without element-scoped transitions runs a boundary scope as a document transition.
61.5 [x] Guard: docs-features `named content still` opens a doc page from a home card and finds no element inside a `view-transition-name` element with a computed animation. Red with HEAD's doc.page ("div.animate-rise rise") on node-dev and node-prod chromium, green 18 of 18 with the fix (3 runs, with `css module` and `reset`). A Playwright check of the paint itself is not possible here: Playwright's headed Firefox video is blank on this Wayland session, and headless does not composite like headed.
61.6 [x] Verify: the fixed docs app in the system Firefox 155: 4 card navigations monotone, flips 0, no pulse.
61.7 [ ] To file after release (manual, per the user): the Firefox report draft .tmp/grunt/p14/b61/bugzilla.md (this entry) the Playwright Juggler stall draft .tmp/grunt/p14/b34-upstream.md (B34), and the Vite module runner draft .tmp/grunt/p14/b73/vite-upstream.md (B73), all unfiled. The solid-query item (B39) is done: it already existed upstream as TanStack/query#11903, and our variant is a comment there, https://github.com/TanStack/query/issues/11903#issuecomment-6160273360.

62 [x] B62 A pending read in the new page held the navigation while the URL had already moved
62.1 [x] Repro (prod, Chromium and Firefox; flare-6f's probe .tmp/grunt/p14/i18n-probe.mjs): from /hr/docs/styling, the sidebar link to /hr/docs/i18n. The URL changed at about 130 ms, while the title and the one active link stayed on Styling for 7.9 s, until the feedback query failed. A click on Middleware in that window threw an uncaught error and never showed Middleware; a click on Head and SEO then showed two active links.
62.2 [x] Cause, measured in a unit probe on Solid 2 rc.14. The doc route is one page with a `[slug]` param, and the outlet kept a page mounted across a param change. Its feedback `<Loading>` had already revealed, so the new slug's pending read held the whole commit (Solid's transition hold: a revealed boundary keeps its content). A fresh page shows its own fallback at once, which the probe confirmed. Meanwhile the navigator wrote history right after the view-transition update returned, which a held commit does too, so the URL moved ahead of the screen, and a second navigation interleaved with the held one.
62.3 [x] Fix (B106). (1) The outlet keys a page slot on its params as well as its id, so a page with new params remounts and its reads start under fresh boundaries that show their fallbacks; layouts and a search-only change stay mounted. (2) `store.commit` returns whether that snapshot reached the screen: an effect over the store's signal runs only when a commit is shown, and a held commit that a newer one replaces resolves false. The navigator writes history and scroll only once its commit is shown, and a replaced one writes nothing and resolves (B105). Popstate, the overlay, and shallow moves keep their order: the browser moved first, or nothing can hold.
62.4 [x] Tests: navigate-slots `pending read commits` (same page, new param, pending read: the page shows its fallback and the push happens with it) and `supersede pending read` (a held commit moves no URL; a second navigation commits, both resolve, no rejection), each red at HEAD; outlet `page remounts` gains a params change, red at HEAD; docs-navigation `pending read` holds the feedback GETs with `page.route` and samples every frame: red at HEAD on node-dev and node-prod chromium and node-prod-firefox (the URL was /hr/docs/i18n while Styling showed), green 27 of 27 with `active link` and `i18n page` (3 runs).
64 [x] B64 /hr/docs/i18n failed with "Something broke" after its feedback query retried for 7 s
64.1 [x] Repro (prod, Chromium and Firefox; flare-6f's probe .tmp/grunt/p14/i18n-probe.mjs): from /hr/docs/styling, the sidebar link to /hr/docs/i18n. The feedback server function answered 400 four times (at 0.1, 1.1, 3.1, and 7.1 s, TanStack's retries), then the whole page became "Something broke / Invalid input". A direct load showed the same.
64.2 [x] Cause, two parts. (a) Framework, B107: `serverFnQueryOptions` left TanStack's default retry on, so a deterministic 4xx rejection was retried 3 times before the error showed. (b) Docs app: feedback.ts checked the slug with `/^[a-z-]{1,40}$/`, which rejects "i18n", and the widget had no `<Errored>` of its own, so its failure reached the page's boundary. The 7 s window is also what B106 (62) measured: the pending read held the navigation.
64.3 [x] Fix. (a) `serverFnQueryOptions` returns a `retry` that fails a server-function rejection with a 4xx status at once and keeps TanStack's default otherwise: 3 retries in a browser, none on the server. An app's own `retry` on the options wins. (b) The feedback input accepts exactly the slugs the doc route serves (`docOf`), and `Feedback` sits in its own `<Errored>` with a one-line fallback, so a failing widget leaves the page.
64.4 [x] Tests: options.test `no retry on 4xx` (a 400 makes 1 call, a network failure and a 500 make 4, the app's `retry: 2` makes 3, and on the server nothing retries); red at HEAD (the network case made 1 call under Node, where query-core's own server default applied). docs-navigation `i18n page` (a direct load and a sidebar navigation each render the title and the tally, with no "Something broke"): red at HEAD on node-dev and node-prod chromium and node-prod-firefox (3 of 3), green 9 of 9 (3 runs).
65 [x] B65 Back showed home at the top under the old doc page, then jumped to the saved position
65.1 [x] Report (real Chrome, the mouse's Back button): from a scrolled /docs/defer to home, the transition showed home at scrollY 0 with the old doc content drawn over everything, the header included, then jumped to the restored scroll. flare-6f's probe (.tmp/grunt/p14/back-scroll-probe.mjs, prod): a card click from home at 563 committed the doc page inside the view-transition update with scrollY still 563 and reset it to 0 only after the update returned; Firefox captured the new page at 564 and jumped at the next frame.
65.2 [x] Cause, framework (B108): the navigator scrolled (to the hash or the top, or the Back restore) after the transition's update returned, so the new snapshot showed the old position and the page jumped after it. With B106 the scroll had moved later still, after the commit was confirmed on screen. A saved position deeper than the page could scroll at commit time was restored once, as far as it went.
65.3 [x] Cause, docs app: the doc pages' `#article` is a named group (`view-transition-name: article`) and home has none, so on Back the article exists only in the old state. Its old snapshot paints above the root group, at its old scroll offset, so the text the sticky header used to cover draws over the header while it fades. Seen in Chromium screencast frames: the old "Params" text and code block over the header and the cards for about 6 frames.
65.4 [x] Fix. Framework: the page being left keeps its position before the DOM changes, and the scroll runs inside the update right after the commit, when that commit shows at once (`store.onScreen()`); a commit Solid holds scrolls when it shows, and history still moves only then (B106). A restore that cannot reach its position yet retries each time the page grows (a ResizeObserver on the document element), until it is reached, the user scrolls, types, or taps, the next navigation scrolls, or 10 s pass. Docs app: `::view-transition-old(article):only-child { display: none }`, so an exit-only article leaves at once; Chromium and Firefox both support `:only-child` on the pseudo and apply it. It hides an exit, so it cannot bring back B61's entrance flicker. dx/navigate.md notes the pattern.
65.5 [x] Tests: scroll.test `scroll inside transition` (a stub startViewTransition records scrollY when its update returns: 0 forward from 500, 500 on Back; red at HEAD with 500 forward), `restore grows`, and `page growth`; docs-navigation `back keeps scroll` (the transition's `ready` and first frame read 0 forward and the saved 500 on Back, no jump after, and the exit-only article computes `display: none`): red at HEAD on node-dev, node-prod, and workers-prod chromium and node-prod-firefox (`ready 500` forward), and `block` without the CSS; green 36 of 36 and then 24 of 24.
65.6 [x] Measure (Chromium screencast, Back from a doc page scrolled to 400 to home at 500, 3 rounds): header pixels outside the range a crossfade can produce, worst per frame 4.7 to 6.4% as is, 2.5% with the exit-only article hidden. The remaining 2.5% is the translucent header's own crossfade over different content behind it.
68 [x] B68 A layout whose own params changed still held a navigation on a pending read
68.1 [x] Found with B106 (BUGS.md 62): the outlet keyed a layout slot by match id only, so a layout stayed mounted when its own segment's params changed (the `[[locale]]` root on a locale switch), and a read keyed on those params under a boundary it had already revealed held the whole commit, as a page did before 62.
68.2 [x] Fix (flare-6f's ruling, with one exception): a layout slot's key gains the params its own segment introduces, the param names in its id that the layout above it does not name. A layout whose own params change remounts, and its reads show their fallbacks; a layout whose own params are unchanged stays mounted. The exception is the root: it renders the document (`<html>`, `<head>`, `<body>`), which a client remount cannot rebuild. Keying it too left the old English DOM in place while the URL moved to /hr (docs-features `rtl client`, `tabs sync`, `set locale persists` and docs-navigation `locale switch` failed on node-dev, node-prod, and workers-prod chromium, 12 of 12). So the root stays mounted, and on a locale switch the page under it remounts with the new params; a read in the root itself keyed on its params can still hold, but since 62 the URL waits for it, so the screen and the URL agree.
68.3 [x] Test: outlet `layout own params` (a layout's own `[id]` changes while it reads a value keyed on it under its own `<Loading>`, below a `[[locale]]/_root_` whose `locale` changes too: the fallback shows, and the root does not remount), red at HEAD (the layout held `L`). The locale e2e cases pass again: 18 of 18 with `pending read` and `i18n page`.
69 [x] B69 `defer two skeletons` asserted a skeleton that a slow runner had already replaced
69.1 [x] CI run 38505916542 (cf2fcdb1), e2e-mac node-dev-webkit docs-data `defer two skeletons`: `#stats-loading` was not found. flare-6f's attribution: on the slow mac dev runner `#releases` became visible after `stats` (900 ms) had already settled, so the skeleton was rightly gone. Not a product bug.
69.2 [x] Fix (test side, as B55): `stats` is held on the server until the test has seen its skeleton beside the released list, then released, capped at 10 s, so the case asserts the real contract (the fast boundary does not wait for the slow one) on any machine. The changelog is an ISR page, whose shared render sees no search and no request headers (dx/cache.md), so a `?hold` query or a cookie cannot reach its loader; the docs app has a per-process gate instead (`src/stats-gate.ts`, a server-only `statsGate` the loader awaits) with two server functions the test calls (`holdStats`, `releaseStats` in `src/fns/stats-gate.ts`). Each docs worker runs its specs serially, so one gate per server process is safe.
69.3 [x] Verify: with `releases` at 1200 ms, after `stats`, as on the slow runner, HEAD's test fails 2 of 2 and the new one passes 2 of 2 (node-prod-chromium). The changelog cases (`defer two skeletons`, `defer rejects`, `stream fill`, `tailwind critical`, `stylesheet after critical`, `rapid links`) pass 78 of 78 on node-dev chromium and firefox, node-prod, bun-prod, and workers-prod chromium (3 runs).
69.4 [x] The gate's server functions are public POST endpoints in an app that can be deployed (plan 13.11), so `holdStats` holds only under the e2e boot: boot.ts sets `FLARE_E2E=1` in the env of the Node and Bun dev and prod servers and as a Miniflare binding for workerd, and `e2eBoot(env)` reads it from the fetch env or the process env. Elsewhere `holdStats` answers `{ held: false }` and changes nothing; the e2e case asserts it got `held: true`. tests/integration/docs.test `stats gate refused` checks the guard; the changelog cases pass 26 of 26 on the five projects.
69.5 [x] The gate polls (2026-10-15): `statsGate` checks `statsHold.held` every 10 ms on the held render's own timer, capped at 10 s, and `releaseStats` only clears the flag, as `buildLog`'s gate does (BUGS.md 100), so no promise crosses requests. The old cross-request version passed 15 of 15 on workerd prod and dev, so this is consistency, not a fix of a failure. Red: `defer two skeletons` with its release removed fails 3 of 3 at `#stats` (5 s expect against the 10 s cap); the new gate passes 18 of 18 on node, bun, and workers in all three engines.

73 [x] B73 Dev requests failed with "has no route record" while a route module re-evaluated after an edit
73.1 [x] Found with B59: saving the docs app's `styles.css` while pages load in a loop made concurrent dev requests fail, 10 errors in one run of six edits, some reading `route` of undefined. The docs root layout imported `localeConfig` from `router.ts`, which imports `routes.gen.ts`, whose lazy `import()` loads the root layout: a cycle that closes only through the dynamic edge. Vite 8.1.4's module runner (`cachedRequest`, `isCircularRequest`) answers an import of a module still evaluating whose imports reach the caller's call stack with its partial exports instead of waiting, so the generated `.then((loaded) => loaded.route)` read `undefined`.
73.2 [x] Fix (flare-77's ruling): the generated lazy ref returns the namespace, `() => import("../routes/<file>")`, and the router's `load()` reads `route` from it, in `shared/router/load-chain.ts`, which takes `loadChain` and `loadedModule` out of `create-router.ts` (300 lines). In dev only, a namespace whose `route` is not set yet is checked once per macrotask for at most 600 ms, since the live binding fills when the evaluation finishes; production reads it at once, and a missing record rejects with "<id> has no route record: export `route`". The blind import retry tried first was dropped. The docs app moves `localeConfig` to a leaf module, `src/locale-config.ts`, which `router.ts` re-exports, and dx/routes.md "Lazy route modules" tells apps to keep shared config in a leaf module.
73.3 [x] Test: load-chain `namespace record` (a namespace with `route`, one filled 5 ms later in dev, one never filled, and a late fill outside dev, which rejects: a waiting loader would see the fill), red at HEAD; codegen `one route` expects the bare import.
73.4 [x] Verify: under the same six edits, HEAD's loader with the cycle gave 10 errors; the fix with the cycle put back gave none of the missing-record kind; the fixed docs app gave 0.
73.5 [ ] To file after release (manual): the Vite report draft .tmp/grunt/p14/b73/vite-upstream.md. A minimal app outside Flare did not reproduce it in 20 minutes (.tmp/grunt/p14/b73/vite-repro.mjs).
76 [x] B76 Workers dev rendered every request on Node, not in workerd
76.1 [x] Found in plan step 3.4: the e2e suite on `workers-dev-chromium` and `workers-dev-firefox` (a scratch config; the Linux grid has no `*-dev` projects but node's until 4.2) failed `store per runtime`: the docs app answered `x-docs-store: fs`, so `caches.default` was missing where it rendered. A probe copy of `minimal` logging from its server module showed every request served with no `caches` and a Node user agent. Since 31efb227 (B95 (b)) flare's `configureServer` runs first (`order: "pre"`, so devHead starts before the Cloudflare plugin's worker waits for it). Vite registers each hook's returned post hook in that order, so flare's Node listener sat ahead of the Cloudflare plugin's post middleware, which dispatches to workerd, and answered every request. So B59's "Workers dev writes the same three styles" (59.4) was Node's answer.
76.2 [x] Fix: under `workersDev`, flare's server hook still relays the worker environment's errors and answers `flare:styles`, but returns no listener, so workerd answers. Without it, nothing changes.
76.3 [x] With workerd answering, `defer two skeletons` failed on workers-dev: `holdStats` refused, because Workers dev builds the worker's env from the Wrangler config and dotenv files only, so boot.ts's `FLARE_E2E=1` never reached it (69.4). boot.ts now sets `CLOUDFLARE_INCLUDE_PROCESS_ENV=true` for the Workers dev boot, which makes the Cloudflare plugin add the process env to the worker's env.
76.4 [x] Test: flare.test `workers dev server` (with `workersDev: true` the server hook returns no post hook, uses no middleware, and still listens for the worker environment's `flare:error`), red before the fix. The probe after the fix: every request served with `caches` present (`[object CacheStorage]`, user agent `Cloudflare-Workers`).
76.5 [x] Guard (flare-77's ruling): the minimal app's middleware sets `x-runtime` from `navigator.userAgent`, and boot.test asserts `Cloudflare-Workers` for `workers dev` and `workers prod` and `Bun/…` for `bun dev` and `bun prod`; with the bug put back, `workers dev` fails with `Node.js/24`, so the unit gate now catches it.
76.6 [x] Verify (plan 3.4): the whole e2e suite on workers-dev chromium and firefox, 269 passed, 0 failed, 31 skipped, `store per runtime`, `defer two skeletons`, `dev log clean`, `dev first paint`, `dev styles adopted`, `hydrate`, and the basepath cases among them; on bun-dev chromium and firefox (the run before this fix, which touches only Workers dev) every case passed. The runs set `TMPDIR` off the shared tmpfs, whose per-user quota other sessions had filled (a dep-optimizer write failed with `Disk quota exceeded`).
79 [x] B79 WebKit fetched every shared chunk twice on a cold load
79.1 [x] CI run 38516712348 (60e09196), e2e-mac workers-prod-webkit docs-smoke `every page hydrates /changelog`: 12 WebKit warnings that a preloaded resource was not used within a few seconds of load, the stylesheet and 11 JS chunks. Reproduced locally with Playwright WebKit 2311 (WPE) behind a counting proxy, /changelog open 6 s: the same 12 warnings and hashes. The 11 chunks are the client entry's static imports, fetched as module scripts at 180 ms, so hydration uses them. At 189 ms the lazy route import (Vite's `__vitePreload`) added modulepreload links for its chunk's dependencies, those 11 among them. Chromium and Firefox skip a modulepreload for a module already in the module map; WebKit fetches it again: 34 asset requests for 23 files on node-prod (11 full refetches, no cache headers) and on workers-prod (11 revalidations answering 304 under the assets binding's `public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`); with assets forced `immutable`, 23 for 23 and no JS warnings. The stylesheet's warning is 81.
79.2 [x] Fix (flare-77's ruling): `vite/plugin/preload.ts`, a build plugin, sets the client environment's `build.modulePreload.resolveDependencies` to drop every dependency in the client entry's static import closure, which is always evaluated before any dynamic import runs. A `generateBundle` hook ordered `pre` reads the closure from the bundle before Vite's own resolves the lists, starting at the chunk whose facade module is the client entry: at that point Rolldown marks each lazy import's chunk as an entry too.
79.3 [x] Test: preload `entry closure not preloaded` (a real client build: no lazy import's list names a chunk of the entry's closure, and the routes' own chunks stay listed), red at HEAD (2 closure chunks listed); `entry closure`.
79.4 [x] Verify: /changelog on workers-prod, WebKit: 23 asset requests for 23 files (34 before), only the stylesheet's warning left; Chromium unchanged except for 80's two prefetch repeats.
80 [x] B80 The idle prefetch fetched the current page's own chunks again
80.1 [x] Found with B79's probe: on /changelog, Chromium and Firefox fetched the page's chunk and its layout's twice, 41 asset requests for 39 files. The router's `modules: "all"` appended a `rel=prefetch` link for every chunk in `router.chunks` after idle, the current chain's among them, which hydration had imported 36 ms before. A prefetch goes to the HTTP cache, not the module map, so each was a second request: a full refetch without cache headers, a 304 under the Workers assets defaults. WebKit does not support `rel=prefetch`, so it made none.
80.2 [x] Fix (flare-77's ruling): `all()` skips every chunk file that holds a module the client has imported (`loadedModule`), so the hydrated chain and anything a navigation or prefetch already loaded is not fetched again.
80.3 [x] Test: prefetch `loaded chunks skipped` (lazy root, home, about, and slow, slow sharing home's file; after `/` loads, only about's file is prefetched), red at HEAD (all three files). `all modules` and `save data` now expect a loaded or bundled module's file to be left out.
81 [x] B81 WebKit's stylesheet preload notice failed the console guard
81.1 [x] The twelfth warning of CI run 38516712348 (79.1): WebKit says `/assets/style-*.css` "was preloaded using link preload but not used within a few seconds from the window's load event". B15 loads the one stylesheet as a preload whose `rel` a nonced script flips to `stylesheet`. Behind a counting proxy the file reaches the network once on every engine, with no cache headers, under the Workers assets defaults, and with `immutable`; WebKit's second stylesheet request is a cache hit. WebKit does not count a preload consumed by a `rel` flip, so the notice is engine noise, shown on any page open a few seconds after load.
81.2 [x] Ruling (flare-77): keep B15's delivery, the CSP-safe high-priority path; a `media="print"` flip would trade a real priority cost for a cosmetic warning. The e2e console guards (docs.fixture `watch`, navigation, static) ignore only a message with that WebKit text naming the built stylesheet, through `tests/e2e/console.ts`; any other preload warning, a JS chunk's among them, still fails.
81.3 [x] Test: docs-smoke `preloads settle` (a prod `/changelog` left open 5 s past network idle logs nothing), red on local WebKit node-prod and workers-prod before the filter (only the stylesheet notice, since 79), green after; with `every page hydrates`, `click through`, and minimal `hydrate`, 63 of 63 on WebKit node-prod and workers-prod and Chromium workers-prod.
81.4 [x] Resolved by 87: with the stylesheet link consuming the preload, WebKit logs no "preloaded but not used" notice (0 on /changelog after 6 s, 0 in 243 prod WebKit cases), so `tests/e2e/console.ts` and its filter are gone and the guards treat every warning as a problem again.

85 [x] B85 Two Workers dev servers started together raced for the inspector port
85.1 [x] Found replaying CI's e2e-linux job inside the pinned image (2ed93b01): a workers-dev boot exited with `listen EADDRINUSE: address already in use 127.0.0.1:9230`, after "Default inspector port 9229 not available, using 9230 instead"; the local grid hit the same on 9235. The Cloudflare plugin resolves its inspector port with get-port, 9229 or the first free one after, and binds it later: two dev servers starting together both pick the same free port, and the one that binds second crashes. Any user running two `vite dev` servers with `workersDev` at once can hit it. With the port fixed, a fresh-worktree gate then failed `workers dev twice` on `workerd/util/sqlite.c++:844: SQLite failed; database is locked: SQLITE_BUSY`: the plugin persists the worker's state by default at `.wrangler/state/v3` in the app, which two dev servers of one app share, and every Workers dev boot wrote that, and a `cf.json` cache, into the app (against B70).
85.2 [x] Fix (flare-77's ruling, option (ii), widened): `workersDev` takes `{ inspectorPort?: number | false; persistState?: boolean | { path: string } }` as well as `true`, both passed through to the plugin. Absent, the plugin's default stays (9229 for chrome://inspect); `0` asks the OS for a port, which Miniflare binds itself, so no two servers can race for it; `false` turns it off. `persistState: false` keeps the state in memory. Anything else throws `workersDev`. The e2e apps (`docs`, `minimal`, `basepath`) pass `{ inspectorPort: 0, persistState: false }` under the Workers dev boot, and boot.ts sets `CLOUDFLARE_CF_FETCH_ENABLED=false` for it, so a boot writes nothing into the app. B95 carries the amendment.
85.3 [x] Test: boot.test `workers dev twice` (two minimal Workers dev boots via Promise.all both answer 200), red at HEAD 2 of 3 runs with `EADDRINUSE 127.0.0.1:9229` and in a fresh worktree with `SQLITE_BUSY`; `workers dev` asserts no `.wrangler` in the app afterwards, red with the plugin's default persistence; workers-dev `inspector port`; options `workers dev option`. After the fix, the pair passed 6 of 6 over 3 runs, and no app has a `.wrangler`.
86 [x] B86 A dev document lost its styles when the server modules reloaded
86.1 [x] Leaf 4.6 (a): under the 18-project grid at 8 workers, `dev log clean` saw `[flare:styles] no dev styles from the dev server within 2000 ms`, and `dev first paint` saw an unstyled body (margin 8px). Timed on both sides over the 9 dev projects at 8 workers: the dev server's walk p50 0 ms, p90 47, max 205; a request reached it at most 17 ms after it was sent; answered round trips p50 6 ms, p90 33, p99 142, max 218. Yet 4 requests timed out at 2 s. With request and client ids in the trace, a timed-out request had been received and answered in 126 ms, and its client's listener never heard the reply.
86.2 [x] Cause: Vite's full reload (a file edit, a dependency re-optimized) re-evaluates the server modules, and the new evaluation's hot context removes the previous one's custom listeners (`HMRContext`'s constructor in Vite 8.1.4's module runner). A document still rendering on the old evaluation had its request in flight; the reply reached only the new evaluation's listener, whose own map did not hold it, so the document waited out the bound and rendered without dev styles. The 2 s bound was not too tight: no answered request came near it. Clients were also many per server process (most made 1 to 20 requests), each numbering from 0.
86.3 [x] Fix: the requests in flight live in the hot context's `data`, which Vite keeps for a module across its re-evaluations, so the newest listener settles any of them and request numbers never repeat. The dev styles client (`devStyles`) is made once per evaluation of `create.ts`, while it evaluates, so its listener is in place in the same turn the reload's new context drops the old one; `createServer` still reads dev mode per call. The bound stays 2 s.
86.4 [x] Test: server/render/dev-styles `reload` (a request before a reload is settled by the new listener; two clients number 0 and 1), red at HEAD; projector `dev styles sink` (two sinks over one `data` number on).
86.5 [x] Verify: the 9 dev projects at 8 workers, 1218 passed, 0 failed, no `[flare:styles]` warning (4 timeouts in the run before the fix).
87 [x] B87 WebKit failed loads after B15's swap cancelled the stylesheet preload
87.1 [x] Leaf 4.6 (b), and CI run 38529555984 (e2e-linux shard 2/3, bun-prod-webkit `every page hydrates /examples/a/b?tab=data`, 3x "Failed to load resource: WebKit encountered an internal error"): under parallel load a prod WebKit page sometimes failed to load its client entry (no hydration, 30 s), an image, icon.svg, or the stylesheet. Each failing trace's network log (9 of 9 over 3 runs) starts the same way: the stylesheet preload, `Load request cancelled`, then loads that fail with WebKit's internal error. B15's swap script flipped the preload's `rel` to `stylesheet` while the preload was still in flight; WebKit cancels the preload and starts a new stylesheet load, and under load the loads after that cancel failed. Not the static server: node-prod and bun-prod both failed, the instrumented Bun server answered every asset in 2 ms or less, and alone the page passed 40 of 40. At normal load the cancel cost no extra network request (a 500 ms slow stylesheet: 2 browser requests, 1 network hit).
87.2 [x] Fix (flare-77's ruling, a B15 amendment): the nonced script leaves the preload alone and adds a `<link rel="stylesheet">` right after itself, its `href` attribute copied as written from the preload (`getAttribute`, so the markup stays root-relative, as docs-features `tailwind critical` and `stylesheet after critical` select it), so the new link takes the preloaded response. The text stays constant (a block keeps its binding off the global scope), the `<noscript>` link is unchanged, and the order after `#flare-critical` holds (B103).
87.3 [x] Test: delivery `swap keeps the preload` (jsdom runs the markup: the preload keeps its rel, one stylesheet link after the script with the same href), red at HEAD; `script constant`; stylesheet `prod` and nonce `stylesheet` carry the new text.
87.4 [x] Verify: the same WebKit load (node-prod, bun-prod, workers-prod; 16 pages; 8 workers), HEAD's swap 7 of 864 failed, all with the cancelled preload, against 0 of 864 with the new one. One network fetch of the stylesheet on Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit behind the counting proxy (`/changelog`, workers-prod). Render blocking unchanged: with the stylesheet delayed 1 s, the first frame and FCP came at about 107/116 ms on Chromium and 284/301 ms on Firefox before the stylesheet ended (1072, 1311 ms), with the old swap and the new alike; on WebKit the first frame waited for the stylesheet with both swaps (1038 old, 1031 new, stylesheet end 1012), so B15's non-blocking stylesheet does not hold on WebKit either way. WebKit prod specs with no console filter: 243 passed, no preload notice. The full 18-project grid: 931 passed, 0 crashes, one failure, 89's.
89 [x] B89 The post-hydrate fill commit was held silently behind its own pending values
89.1 [x] Leaf 4.6 (d), and CI run 38532044099 (bun-dev-webkit): `every page hydrates /changelog` and `dev log clean` failed on Solid's `[SILENT_HOLD] writes to "createRouterStore.snapshot" … held 207–556ms waiting on "computed"`. Solid's attribution, enabled for a probe, showed it on every dev load of `/changelog`, an ISR page with `defer: "stream"`: one silent hold of the router snapshot, 108 ms on WebKit at rest, past Solid's 200 ms console threshold under load. After hydrate the client fills the stored page's holes with `?_flare=fill`, a same-URL revalidation that committed at `r`, while the values it carried were still pending; the commit was held until they landed, with nothing on screen acknowledging the wait.
89.2 [x] Ruled and dropped: skipping the store-resolved values in the fill (`latest`, `prerender: "resolve"`) and keeping them on the client. A stored page's state carries a placeholder for every deferred value when it has holes, and the client's only source for a store-resolved one is the fill: with the skip, `latest`'s slot never resolved, rejected at the fill's end, and the root showed "Something broke". Keeping the on-screen wrappers for the holes broke differently (the hole reads update only when the fill's commit hands them new wrappers). Measured variants that committed with a hole pending held 869–885 ms and held `broken`'s error signal too, so the error showed at about 900 ms instead of 300. The skip, kept as a patch (.tmp/grunt/p14/b89/isHole.patch), returns with 94.
89.3 [x] Fix: the fill commits once its response has ended. `go()` waits for the landing's end (`endOf`, which frames.ts records where the frames after `r` are read) outside `navigation.track`, so a background fill shows no progress, and re-checks that no newer navigation started. The hole values still settle through their slots as their frames arrive; only the store write waits, and nothing it reads is pending.
89.4 [x] Test: navigate-fill `fill commits at its end` (a fill body left open after `r`: the snapshot keeps the stored data until the body ends), red without the wait; the fill cases move to navigate-fill.test.ts. docs-smoke `fill holds nothing` (a `/changelog` load kept open 1.5 s logs nothing).
89.5 [x] Verify: Solid's hold history on a dev `/changelog` load, one hold of 1 ms on Chromium and on WebKit (108 ms on WebKit at HEAD); every value renders.
93 [x] B93 Workers dev's render error line reached the captured log after the test had read it
93.1 [x] CI run 38532044099 (7f889273), workers-dev-webkit docs-smoke `dev log clean`: the declared `[flare:render] … The stats service is down` line was missing from the captured log (received []). Since 76, workerd renders in Workers dev, so the server's console no longer writes to the dev server's own stderr.
93.2 [x] Cause: the Cloudflare plugin hands workerd's console to Miniflare's structured log handler, which writes it through Vite's logger to the dev server's output, the stream the boot captures. That happens after the response, asynchronously, while `dev log clean` read the log as soon as its last page closed. Nothing drops the line: it arrives late. Locally 0 of 8 failed at HEAD, so the race needs CI's slower WebKit host.
93.3 [x] Fix, with B109 (the app composes `cloudflare({ viteEnvironment: { name: "ssr" } })` itself, revising 76's and 85's option shape): `dev log clean` polls the captured flare lines for 10 s until they equal the declared ones, then checks for unparsed HTML and uncaught errors. A line no page declares still shows as itself.
93.4 [x] Test: boot.test `workers dev log` (`docs` in Workers dev, the changelog's fill runs its failing `broken` hole; the `[flare:render]` line reaches the boot's capture through Miniflare). `vite/plugin/cloudflare.test.ts` covers the composition, and boot.test `workers prod` now runs Miniflare from the plugin's generated `<outDir>/server/wrangler.json`, what `wrangler deploy` would ship.
93.5 [x] B109 cost, measured: the docs app's Workers production build takes about 2.0 s with prerender (39 seeds) and about 1.18 s without (3 runs each), so the Node build that prerender renders from, plus the renders, adds about 0.8 s. Plan leaf 12.4, after the release: evaluate prerendering a Workers app through the plugin's `vite preview` in workerd, so a loader that needs a Workers binding can prerender.
93.6 [x] Reopened (flare-77, CI run 38551698134 at 36c0c703, e2e-mac shard 2): workers-dev-webkit `dev log clean` polled 10 s and received no `[flare:` line at all, so on macOS the declared line did not reach the captured log. Not deterministic: mac shard 2 of CI run 38553304044 (f805a74f) passed. Not reproduced here: workers-dev-webkit's whole suite on one worker, as e2e-mac runs it, 136 of 136; `dev styles adopted` before `dev log clean` on one boot, without and with 99's fix, 6 of 6. The e2e-mac job uploaded nothing, so the macOS server log was never seen.
93.7 [x] Diagnostics: a failing docs case writes the server lines logged while it ran to `server-log.txt` beside its trace (`docs.fixture.ts`; a forced failure on workers-dev-chromium wrote the 17 lines, the `[flare:render]` one among them), and both e2e jobs upload `packages/core/test-results` on failure (ci.test `ci failure artifacts`). The next macOS failure carries the log, which settles the line's form there.
93.8 [x] The first macOS artifact (CI run 38562711566, e2e-mac-2, workers-dev-webkit `dev log clean`): `server-log.txt` was empty, so the boot captured no server line during the whole case, not a line in another form. Next probe (flare-77): the docs server logs `[docs:probe] server up` at its first request (a `[docs:` prefix, outside `dev log clean`'s `[flare:` set), the first case of each boot requests `/maintenance` and requires that line in the boot's log within 2 s, and a failure attaches the whole boot's log too (`server-session-log.txt`). That tells a capture that misses workerd's console on macOS apart from a render that did not run. Locally the line reaches the capture on all 18 projects; with the expected text changed, the first case fails with `the boot's log has no …` and both logs attached.
93.9 [x] Attributed (CI run 38570893250, e2e-mac-2): the probe passed on mac's Workers dev boot, and its session log has `[flare:render] The stats service is down` five times, each from an earlier case on the same boot, while `dev log clean`'s own slice is empty. flare's render log does not de-duplicate (`projectError` logs each projection), so that case's `/changelog` visit never ran `broken`. Cause, the test: `broken` runs only in the post-hydrate fill, which starts right after `data-flare-hydrated` is set, and `page.waitForLoadState("networkidle")` returns at once when the page already reached network idle. On a slow engine hydration comes more than 500 ms after the last module fetch, so network idle fires first, the case closes the page as soon as the document has hydrated, and the fill never runs. Repro: an init script that holds the main thread 1 s before `data-flare-hydrated` is set: workers-dev-webkit `dev log clean` failed 2 of 2 with CI's `Received []` after its 10 s poll (node-dev-chromium passed: Node runs the fill even when its tab closes). Fix: a page entry names the element that proves it rendered the value that logs (`shows`, `#broken-error` for `/changelog`), and the case waits for it before closing the page. Under the same 1 s hold, 9 of 9 on workers-dev-webkit, node-dev-chromium, and bun-dev-firefox.
98 [x] B98 `abort keeps latest` delayed only a request the navigation never made
98.1 [x] CI run 38541088168 (e89f9780), e2e-mac workers-dev-webkit docs-resilience `abort keeps latest`: the case clicks Auth, whose `?_flare=data` is delayed 800 ms, then Styling, and `#doc-title` read "Auth" for the whole 5 s. Locally 1 of 540 at 84801b61 (all 18 projects ×30, bun-dev-firefox), and 2 of 540 on the 9 dev projects ×60 (workers-dev-webkit).
98.2 [x] Cause: Playwright hovers before it clicks, so each click first prefetches (`?_flare=prefetch`), and the navigation reuses that request. The route held only `?_flare=data`, which never went out: Auth landed in about 50 ms and started its view transition. A probe logged each click's target, the navigator's arrival and update, and the transition's start and finish: in 3 of 3 failures (bun-dev-firefox ×2, workers-dev-webkit) Styling's click hit `HTML`, between Auth's `vt start` and `vt finished`, so no link was clicked and Styling never navigated. The browser hit-tests the document element while a document view transition is shown. Only Auth arrived and updated, with its signal not aborted: no superseded commit was involved. `::view-transition { pointer-events: none }` did not change WebKit's target (1 of 60 still hit `HTML`), so no stylesheet rule lets a click through a document transition on every engine.
98.3 [x] Fix: the case holds every Auth data request (`**/docs/auth?_flare=*`), the prefetch included, so Auth is still loading when Styling is clicked and no transition is on screen.
98.4 [x] Found on the way: a navigation's only latest check ran before its view transition, and a transition runs its update a frame or more later (about 120 ms on WebKit in the probe). A newer navigation that starts in that window skips the older transition, which runs the older update first, as the spec orders it: the superseded page committed, showed, and wrote a history entry before the newer one replaced it. Fix: the update commits nothing once a newer navigation aborted its signal. Test: navigate-transition `superseded update` (history `["/a", "/b"]` without the check, `["/b"]` with it).
98.5 [x] Verify: `abort keeps latest` on all 18 projects ×60 through the pinned container, 1080 of 1080 passed (1 failure in 540 before the fix).
95 [x] B95 docs-data `stream` raced a transient line count
95.1 [x] CI run 38532044099 (7f889273), node-prod-webkit and workers-prod-webkit, and CI run 38544933916 (84801b61), bun-prod-webkit: `toHaveCount(1)` on `#stream-log li` saw 6. The local grid at 36c0c703 hit it once, on node-prod-chromium.
95.2 [x] Cause: the test, not the stream. The build log yields 6 lines 150 ms apart, and the case asserted the moment exactly one line shows. A poll that comes more than about 150 ms after the first line sees 2 or more, and on slow macOS hosts the whole stream landed before the first poll. The B55 pattern: a transient state read by polling.
95.3 [x] Fix: an observer installed before the page loads records the time each line count first shows. The case asserts the counts arrived 1 to 6 in order and the last at least 600 ms after the first (the server waits at least 750 ms), then `done`. That is the property: lines render as they stream. A slow runner only widens the spread. flare-77 offered a B55-style hold after line 1 as the other way; the observer needs no release call and checks the property itself.
95.4 [x] Bite: a card that buffers the whole stream and then renders the lines a macrotask apart passes the count order and fails the spread (1.6 and 2.2 ms against 600), node-dev and node-prod chromium, 4 of 4.
95.5 [x] Verify: `stream` on all 18 projects ×20 through the pinned container, 360 of 360.
99 [x] B99 A stylesheet edit in dev reloaded every server module under renders in flight
99.1 [x] CI run 38551698134 (36c0c703), bun-dev-firefox docs-smoke `every page hydrates /login`: `Hydration key miss for "00000000000000001000030k02040A1m100000"` (the page's `<main>`) and `…A1m1000030`. Alone, `/login` passed 450 of 450 on the 9 dev projects ×50. The whole suite ×10 on bun-dev firefox and chromium failed 2 other cases instead: `/api/server/create-server` answered 500 with `Context must either be created with a default value or a value must be provided` from `link.tsx`'s `useContext`, and `stream` read `unknown` from the build log's runs map. `dev styles adopted` beside `every page hydrates` ×20 reproduced CI's `/login` with its exact keys.
99.2 [x] Cause: `dev styles adopted`, the one spec that writes into an app, edits the docs app's `styles.css`, which every dev server of every runtime watches. The root layout imports that stylesheet, nothing on the server accepts it, so Vite sends each server environment a `full-reload` (a probe dev server: one per write, and the root layout module a new instance afterwards), and the module runner evaluates every server module again, Solid and flare's own among them. A render in flight then mixes the two module sets: the server's `/login` HTML has hydration keys on the whole layout and none on the page's `<main>`, which rendered on the new Solid; a context its provider created on the old module set is missing (`link.tsx`, `useBlocker`'s `Context can only be accessed under a reactive root`); module state is gone (the build log's runs). Not runtime or engine specific: node-dev-chromium fails the same way under the edits. Separately, the spec's restore wrote back the file it read first, so two dev projects running it together left the other's rule in `styles.css`.
99.3 [x] Fix: `flare:css-update` (`vite/dev/css-update.ts`) takes a plain stylesheet's update as handled in every server environment (`hotUpdate` returns `[]`), so Vite sends the server no full reload. The page still gets the client's CSS update, and the dev styles read the file through the client environment (B104) with their cache cleared on every change. Only the stylesheet's importers evaluate again on their next import, as Vite invalidates them. A CSS module, a `?inline` or `?url` import, and code keep Vite's own update. `dev styles adopted` appends a rule with a name of its own and removes exactly that rule from what the file holds then.
99.4 [x] Test: css-update `css only` (the hook's cases) and `server kept` (a real dev server: no `full-reload` reaches ssr, and a module that does not import the stylesheet keeps its instance; red without the hook on both counts); flare.test `dev server` (flare() includes the plugin).
99.5 [x] Verify: `every page hydrates` on bun-dev firefox and chromium and node-dev-chromium ×5 while `styles.css` changes every 1.5 s: 3 of 240 failed before the fix (`/playground` 500, `Context can only be accessed under a reactive root`, from `useBlocker`), 0 of 240 after. At one edit every 300 ms every page timed out with and without the fix: Tailwind recompiles the stylesheet for the client on each edit, and the dev styles' cache never settles.
99.6 [x] Follow-up: the docs app's server log at 642f8845's grid (bun-dev-chromium `every page hydrates /`, `Context can only be accessed under a reactive root` from `useNavigation`) still had `(ssr) program reload` twice. A dev server with the docs app's own config, every plugin's `hotUpdate` wrapped, showed the sender: Solid's plugin (`@solidjs/vite-plugin`, its hook before `flare:css-update` in the order flare() lists them) sends a runnable server environment `full-reload` itself whenever the update still has modules, and returns `[]`, so flare's hook saw none. The hook now runs first (`order: "pre"`): Solid's then gets no modules and sends nothing. The docs dev server on node and bun, `styles.css` edited twice: only `(client) hmr update /src/styles.css?direct`, no `program reload`. css-update `server kept` lists Solid's plugin before the hook, red with the hook unordered.
100 [x] B100 macOS WebKit rendered a streamed server function's lines in a burst
100.1 [x] CI run 38560596891 (e6464377), e2e-mac node-prod-webkit and workers-prod-webkit docs-data `stream`: the line counts came 1 to 6 in order, but the last 183 ms after the first, where the server spaces them at least 750 ms. Linux streams them: every engine in the pinned image, every runtime.
100.2 [x] Checked, not the cause: the sniffing candidate. A streamed server-function response and a data response already carry `content-type: application/x-ndjson` and `x-content-type-options: nosniff` on node-prod and workers-prod (the security defaults apply to every answer). The server writes the stream incrementally: 6 chunks of 65 to 76 bytes, 150 ms apart (node-prod from 0 ms, workers-prod from 149 ms). A 512-byte sniff buffer would also land the whole 438-byte body at once, not over 183 ms.
100.3 [x] Diagnostics: `stream` also times every body chunk the client decodes after the click (the line reader calls `TextDecoder.decode(chunk, { stream: true })` per chunk), and its assertions print the line and chunk times. On Linux both are 150 ms apart (`lines [0,149,300,450,601,752]`, `chunks [-1ms:75B,149ms:74B,…]`). The next macOS failure says which layer burst: chunks in a burst are the network's delivery to the page, chunks spaced out with lines in a burst the page's rendering. Then the fix, red first.
100.4 [x] CI run 38564625394 (22cc87d5) passed `stream` on macOS, so the failure-only evidence printed nothing. Every run now writes the line and chunk times to `stream-timing.json` beside the case and attaches it, and e2e-mac uploads its test results on a pass too, so each macOS run gives a sample of how close a pass comes.
100.5 [x] First macOS evidence, CI run 38567959054 (f11f0985), workers-prod-webkit `stream` (a pass): `{"lines":[0,0,1,1,1,2],"chunks":["-1ms:438B"]}`. The whole body reached the client's decoder as one 438 B chunk, complete before reading began, so the burst is below the client, in transport: WebKit's network layer on macOS, or the server stack there. Next probe (flare-77): the case also reads the same stream with the test process's own Node fetch and records its chunks beside the browser's (`node`). Six chunks 150 ms apart from Node on the mac runner put it in WebKit; one chunk puts it in the server stack. Locally both read 6 chunks 150 ms apart.

So B100 belongs to GitHub's macOS runners, not to Flare and not to Safari on a real Mac. What inside the runner makes the production click's paint block the main thread is not measured (flare-77 suggests a VM with no GPU). Proposed (flare-77, awaiting the user): `stream` times each line where Flare's client receives it, against the same spacing bar, with a separate untimed check that every line renders in order; `stream` stays as it is until the user decides.
100.23 [x] Resolved (the user's decision, flare-77): `stream` is deterministic, with no stopwatch. The docs app's `buildLog` checks a per-run gate that the e2e-only server functions `holdBuild` and `releaseBuild` drive (refused without FLARE_E2E); the playground takes `?run=`. The case waits until each line is on screen and only then releases the next, so every line shows while the server holds the next, then checks all six in order. Red: the server collecting every line and yielding them at the end, and Flare's client (`client/server-fn/stream.ts`) reading the whole body before handing any value on, each fail on line 1 (`toHaveCount` expected 1, received 0) on node prod chromium, firefox, and webkit, node dev chromium, and workers and bun prod webkit. The gate polls its release count on its own timer, because workerd cancels a request's continuation when another request resolves its promise (its warning: a promise resolved from a different request context). `stream abort` drops its 500 ms wait: once the card reads `aborted` its read loop has ended, and the held run sent exactly two lines. The cause of the old failures was GitHub's macOS runners, measured absent on a real M3 (100.22). The B100 measurement cases (`tests/e2e/docs-probe.test.ts`, `stream-probe.ts`, the docs server's `x-docs-stream-variant` middleware) are removed, and e2e-mac uploads its results on failure only, as e2e-linux does.

104 [x] B104 workers-prod `body limit` lost its 413 to a connection reset
104.1 [x] The local grid at 22cc87d5: workers-prod-chromium docs-server `body limit` failed with `read ECONNRESET` on its 70 KB POST. Alone it passed 150 of 150 (workers, node, and bun prod chromium ×50).
104.2 [x] Cause: workerd's connection handling, not flare. flare answers 413 from `content-length` without reading the body (`server-fn/input.ts`), as a cap should. workerd then closes the connection with the unread body still buffered, and the kernel resets it. A raw-socket probe of the docs app's prod boot: on workers-prod every run reset, after the 413's 923 bytes at once, and with 0 bytes when the client read 50 or 300 ms late, since a reset discards what the client has not read; node-prod kept the connection and gave the 413 at every delay. Cancelling the request body on the 413 changed nothing.
104.3 [x] Fix: the case sends the oversized POST with `Expect: 100-continue` (`node:http`), so the client sends no body until the server asks for one and nothing is left unread. workerd answers the 413 at once, read 300 ms late, 6 of 6; Node sends `100 Continue`, takes the body, and answers 413. workerd sends no `100 Continue` at all (a small body under the header hung 30 s on every workers-prod project), so on Workers only an answer that reads no body comes back, as the cap's does. A plain small POST answers 200 first, so the endpoint works.
104.4 [x] Verify: `body limit` on all 18 projects ×10, 180 of 180.
88 [x] B88 `isr stored` asserted when its store write landed, not that one render served
88.1 [x] Leaf 4.6 (c): under the full grid at 8 workers, node-prod-chromium `isr stored`: the second GET of `/changelog` answered `flare-cache: miss`, where the case expected `hit`. Once in about a dozen grids; also once each on genuine Node 24 (96.2).
88.2 [x] Cause: the test. A miss runs one shared render, and its slot spans the render and its store write so that no second render starts (`server/cache/inflight.ts`, B31, B33); the first request is not delayed by the write. A repeat that arrives after the render but before the write lands joins the slot and is served from that render as `miss`, which dx/cache.md documents. The case read the first answer and then required `hit`, so a store write still pending under load failed it with no second render. Proof: the docs store's `set` delayed 1 s gave `miss` on 5 of 5.
88.3 [x] Fix: the changelog shows its render's request id (`serverCtx().requestId`, as the API page does). The case requires the repeat to be a `hit` or a `miss` with the first answer's id, then polls until a `hit`, also with that id: one render served the page until it was stored.
88.4 [x] Verify: node-prod, bun-prod, and workers-prod chromium ×5, 15 of 15; with the 1 s write, 5 of 5. Bite: with the flight's join removed and the write slow, the repeat comes from a second render and the case fails on the id (`7c2efffb` against `47f4a38e`), 3 of 3.
94 [x] B94 A stored page's fill ran its store-resolved values again
94.1 [x] Follows 89.2 (flare-77's ruling): a stored page's state carried a placeholder for every deferred value, so the post-hydrate fill re-ran every `prerender: "resolve"` value the store write had already run, the changelog's `latest` among them, only to give the client a value Solid had already put in the HTML. Skipping them in the fill alone (89's dropped attempt) broke the page: the client's one source for such a value was the fill, so its slot rejected at the fill's end.
94.2 [x] Fix: a stored render's Placeholder records its fulfilled value and serializes inline (`{ __deferred, key, value }`) in the stored state and the stored NDJSON's `l` frames, so the stored page is the client's one source for it; the stored NDJSON writes `c` and `e` frames only for a value that did not fulfil. A fill runs only the holes (`isHole`, the store write's own rule) and sends every other value as `{ __deferred, key, kept: true }`. The client's `revive` settles an inline value's slot at once and marks it, so a landing's end neither rejects it nor counts it as a hole to fill, and gives a `kept` value the slot the page already holds, the one promise its reads took; a `kept` value with no slot rejects with `The fill kept "<key>" of <route>, which the page does not hold`.
94.3 [x] Test: defer `stored value inline`, `fill runs the holes only`, `store and fill agree`; serve `resolve runs once` (the resolve function runs once across the store write, the data variant, and the fill: red without the fill scope); frames `inline value`, `kept value`, `missing kept`; navigate-fill `fill keeps inline values` (red on the old `revive` with B89's own failure, `The data response ended before this value`); write `resolved defer` now asserts the inline value and no `c` frame. e2e docs-data `defer resolve stored` (the stored state inline, the fill's `kept` and no `"k":"latest"`) and `stream fill` (`#latest` still `1.0.0` after the fill), with `defer two skeletons`, `defer rejects`, and `every page hydrates /changelog`: 198 passed on all 18 projects ×2.
94.4 [x] Cost, the stored `/changelog` hit on node-prod (raw, gzip -9, brotli 11): before 39310, 8802, 7606 B; after 39423, 8820, 7613 B (+113, +18, +7). With a realistic 42.3 KB list (330 entries of varied words) added to `latest`: before 81640, 14748, 12229 B; after 123509, 19638, 14327 B. The duplicate adds 4890 B gzip and 2098 B brotli, 11.6% and 5.0% of the value's raw size, under the 25% bar. gzip's 32 KB window cannot reach the first copy, so there the duplicate costs 82% of the value's own compressed size (5946 B); brotli's larger window brings it to 45% (4623 B).
92 [x] B92 WebKit waits for the stylesheet before its first paint
92.1 [x] From 87.4: with the stylesheet delayed 1 s, Chromium and Firefox painted before it ended and WebKit's first frame waited for it, with B15's swap old and new alike, so the stylesheet B15 adds without blocking still holds WebKit's first paint.
92.2 [x] Measured (docs node-prod `/changelog`, the pinned image's three engines, median of 7; only the swap script's text rewritten, its nonce kept; shift is the summed box change of `#nav`, `main h1`, `#sidebar`, and `footer` between the first frame and full style), the current swap against adding the link after a double `requestAnimationFrame`. Stylesheet delayed 1 s: Chromium FCP 32 ms and Firefox 39 ms either way, full style about 1035 ms, shift 242 and 244 either way; WebKit FCP 1024 ms and shift 0 with the swap, 54 ms and shift 242 with the double rAF. No delay: the double rAF moves Chromium's full style 26 to 65 ms and shift 0 to 40, Firefox's full style 38 to 54 ms (shift 42 either way), and WebKit's FCP 27 to 65 ms, full style 77 to 154 ms, and shift 0 to 40.
92.3 [x] Ruling (flare-77): measured, no change. The double rAF helps only a slow stylesheet on WebKit, where it trades a wait for a 242 px shift, and makes every normal load paint or style later on all three engines with a shift. WebKit's wait buys a first paint that does not shift. dx/head.md states it.

105 [x] B105 The root entry lacked the throw helpers and navigate that 03-surface promises
105.1 [x] Found writing the README (plan leaf 12.3): the packed root exported none of `redirect`, `notFound`, `unauthenticated`, `unauthorized`, `navigate`, `Href`, `LocationRewrite`, and `./server` not `SecurityConfig`, though 03-surface lists them. The helpers existed in `shared/errors` and were never exported (no commit ever touched them in `src/exports/index.ts`); `navigate` had no standalone function at all. The route context carries no helpers, so a v1 app could not throw `notFound()` or `redirect()` from a loader. exports.test `subpath resolves` checked that each subpath resolves, not the names under it.
105.2 [x] Two bugs behind the missing exports, found once the docs app used them: a loader that throws `redirect()` to another page did not type-check (`TS2313: Type parameter 'Id' has a circular constraint`, and every `Link` in the app lost its params), because a page target was found by reading every route module's type, the loader's own among them; and a `.response()` that threw a helper answered 500 (the gate called it synchronously and nothing settled the throw).
105.3 [x] Fix: the root exports the four helpers, `navigate`, `Href`, and `LocationRewrite`, and `./server` `SecurityConfig`. `navigate` is `client/navigation/active.ts`, the booted client's navigation, set by createClient and cleared on dispose; before boot and on the server it rejects. Codegen emits `pageIds`, a plain union of page ids, and `To` reads it, so a target needs no module's type. A `.response()` function runs inside an async wrapper, its throw settles on the run, a `redirect()` answers its status and Location, an HTTP helper its status, and anything else is a projected 500. 03-surface gives each entry an `Exports:` line.
105.4 [x] Test: exports.test `surface names` (red with the eight names); active `active navigate`; create-client `root navigate`; create `response throws` (red: the redirect escaped as a throw); types-gen `page ids`; the types app's `moved` page, whose loader throws all four helpers (red with TS2313). The docs app uses each (B94): `/api/<unknown>` throws `notFound()` and answers 404 with the page's own not-found; `/legacy` throws a typed `redirect()` from its `.response()`; `/admin/audit` throws `unauthenticated()` and `unauthorized()` from a loader over an optional session (e2e docs-routing `catch-all`, `response redirect`, docs-server `auth throws`), excluded from the sitemap.

106 [x] B106 createRouter ignored most of the options dx/router.md documents
106.1 [x] Found with 105: `RouterOptions` had no `caseSensitive`, `trailingSlash`, `scrollRestoration`, `scrollRestorationBehavior`, `scrollRestorationMaxEntries`, `getScrollRestorationKey`, or `routeCacheMaxEntries`, and `createRouter` set fixed defaults for them; it also ignored `cache`, so an app's router-wide client cache, `false` included, had no effect. Every consumer already read the router's fields (the canonical redirect, the matcher, the scroll store with its `key`, the match cache and prefetch).
106.2 [x] Fix: `createRouter` takes them all, each at its dx default (`shared/router/behavior.ts`); the two counts are integers from 1 to 200 and 1 to 500, else the call throws; `cache.client` takes duration strings or `false`; the client passes `getScrollRestorationKey` to the scroll store.
106.3 [x] Test: behavior `router options` (red: the options were dropped) and `case rule`; canonical-redirect `trailing slash option`; create-client `scroll key` (red without the pass-through).

108 [x] B108 createServer ignored `rewrite`
108.1 [x] Found with 106: `dx/server.md` and 02-system step 4 document `createServer({ rewrite })`, and the canonical step took a `rewrite`, but the server options had none and `createServer` never passed one, so an app's rewrite was dropped. The step also did not check that a replacement stays put, which dx requires: a rewrite that changes its replacement again would answer a chain of redirects, or a loop.
108.2 [x] Fix: `ServerOptions` takes `rewrite`, and `createServer` passes it to the canonical step, skipping a path a mount handles (mounts run first). The step runs the function again on the canonicalized replacement and throws when it changes; that and any throw from the function are a projected 500.
108.3 [x] Test: canonical-redirect `rewrite option` (red: `/old` was 404, not 301), `rewrite loop`; `rewrite recanonical` now counts the second call.

110 [x] B110 Bun's body cap ignored createServer's `bodyLimit`
110.1 [x] Found with 106: `createBunFetch(server)` reads `server.bodyLimit`, but the object `createServer` returns had only `fetch`, so `maxRequestBodySize` was always 1 MiB, whatever `createServer({ bodyLimit })` said. A larger limit was unreachable on Bun, which refused the POST first; a smaller one left the platform cap at 1 MiB, above flare's. serve.test passed a hand-made `{ fetch, bodyLimit }`, so it never met the real object. createServer also took any number, though dx gives 1 to 32 MiB.
110.2 [x] Fix: `FlareServer` carries `bodyLimit`, the checked value; createServer throws on a value that is not an integer from 1 to 32 MiB (`server-fn/input.ts` `bodyLimitOf`), and the server-function cap reads the same value.
110.3 [x] Test: bun serve `server limit` (red: `maxRequestBodySize` 1048576, not 2048), through the real createServer, with the range.

111 [x] B111 The dev styles HMR case wrote the shared docs app, and an early stylesheet edit reloaded every page
111.1 [x] CI 38584988010, e2e-linux 3/3: workers-dev-firefox `every page hydrates /` failed with `Loading failed for the module` for the root layout and the home page, and a pageerror. That server's log has `(client) page reload src/styles.css` during its first page load: another worker's `dev styles adopted` had appended its rule to the shared `src/styles.css`, which B70 forbids (the edit came from B59's condition 3), and the reload cancelled Firefox's module loads.
111.2 [x] Why a reload and not a CSS update: while the server renders, flare's dev styles transform `<sheet>?direct` in the client environment. Vite 8.1.4 enters a module that `transformRequest` creates with `isSelfAccepting = false`, and `vite:css-analysis` sets it true only when the transform ends. An edit of the file before then meets a module that accepts nothing and has no importers, a dead end, and Vite sends every client `full-reload`. A first Tailwind compile holds that window open for hundreds of ms. Measured on private copies of the docs app: 2 reloads in 97 edits timed 0 to 1500 ms into a fresh server's first load, both workers-dev-firefox at 300 ms; an edit after hydration was a CSS hot update on all nine dev projects. So a user who edited a stylesheet while the dev server first compiled it lost every open page's state.
111.3 [x] Fix: the dev styles enter each `?direct` module unanalyzed (`ensureEntryFromUrl(url, false)`) before transforming it, so an edit in that window stops there and the page's own stylesheet module takes it (`vite/dev/dev-styles.ts` `directStyle`). The HMR case runs on a private copy of the docs app (`tests/e2e/copy-boot.ts`): the app in the OS temp dir with the repo's dependencies linked, its own dev server, deleted on close. A copy under the boot outputs, inside the dependency directory, served 187 KB of dev styles against the app's 68 KB, as Tailwind's scanner read beyond the app there.
111.4 [x] Test: dev-styles `edit mid compile`, a real Vite server with the stylesheet's transform held open (red: two `full-reload` sends); copy-boot `private copy`; docs-smoke `dev styles hmr` (the edit applies, a window marker survives, the server logs an `hmr update` for `src/styles.css` and no `page reload`) beside the now read-only `dev styles adopted`. Verify: both cases on the nine dev projects ×3, 54 of 54; with the fix, edits 200 to 400 ms into a first load on workers-dev firefox and chromium ×4, 40 of 40 with no reload, 4 of them inside the window (Vite logged `stop propagation because not analyzed` for `styles.css?direct`).

109 [x] B109 An app behind a proxy could not declare it
109.1 [x] Found with 106: dx documented `createServer({ trustProxy })`, but `createServer` had no such option, `createNodeListener` took no options at all (a second argument threw), and `createBunFetch` none either. The listener always ran `toRequest` with hop count 0, so a Node or Bun app behind nginx or a load balancer that ends TLS served itself as plain http: its origin, a redirect's `Location`, and `Strict-Transport-Security` (absent on http) all followed, with redirect loops and cookies at risk. The authenticate context's `clientIp` and `secure` rows named fields no context carries.
109.2 [x] Fix: `createNodeListener(server, { trustProxy })` passes the hop count to `toRequest`; `createBunFetch(server, { trustProxy })` rebuilds a trusted `https` hop's request as https, method and body kept. Both check it through `shared/url/forwarded.ts`: an integer from 0 to 5, and no other key. dx moves `trustProxy` from `createServer` to the adapters, gives `toRequest` its real shape, and moves the context's `clientIp` and `secure` under Planned (plan 15).
109.3 [x] Test: listener `trust proxy` (red: `createNodeListener takes no options`), through a production server, which answers `Strict-Transport-Security` only behind the trusted hop; bun serve `trust proxy` (red: the URL stayed http); forwarded `hops`, `https`.

112 [x] B112 SecurityConfig took two of the nine keys dx documents
112.1 [x] Found with 106: `SecurityConfig` was `Partial<Record<"X-Frame-Options" | "Content-Security-Policy", string | false>>`, and `applySecurity` applied only those two keys. A CSP directives object, `Permissions-Policy`, `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy`, and `X-Powered-By` did not type-check and were never applied, though `buildCsp` already built directives and permissions (nothing passed it either). The directives with no default (`frame-src`, `frame-ancestors`, `form-action`, `media-src`) and the two boolean directives did not exist, Permissions-Policy had no `"self"`, and an origin with a space or `;` was not refused. The server callback got `{ nonce }` only, not `{ request, env, nonce, serverContext }`, and a route's `.security()` called it again with a new nonce, so `ctx.nonce` was not the one sent. A stored page kept only the two keys of its route's config.
112.2 [x] Fix: `SecurityConfig` has all nine keys, with `CspDirectives` and `PermissionsPolicy` (`shared/route-builder/types.ts`). `applySecurity` applies each, and `buildCsp` writes the directives object, the four named-only directives, the booleans, and every Permissions-Policy value. The request makes one nonce candidate; the server callback runs once, after serverContext, with the request's context, and a route's `.security()` merges onto its resolved config with the same candidate. A set built before serverContext (a 400, a canonical redirect) carries an object config, or the defaults for a callback. A stored page keeps every key.
112.3 [x] Test: apply `server security` (red: the callback read `ctx.request` and threw), `every key`, `callback override`; csp `named directives`, `permission values`, `origin quote`.
